
======================================================================
FILE: .pytest_cache/README.md
======================================================================

# pytest cache directory #

This directory contains data from the pytest's cache plugin,
which provides the `--lf` and `--ff` options, as well as the `cache` fixture.

**Do not** commit this to version control.

See [the docs](https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/how-to/cache.html) for more information.



======================================================================
FILE: scripts/debug_home_page.py
======================================================================

"""Debug script: launch the HomePage with fake data.

This script is a development tool. It is NOT part of production.

It builds a HomePage with:

- An in-memory ProjectCatalog with 5 fake projects (the names from
  Prototipagem_UI_14 p. 37), with fixed creation/update dates.
- Three fake flows in the FlowList, so the StartButton can transition
  from grey to green when a project and a flow are selected.
- All 14 HomePage signals connected to print(), so you can see them
  fire in the console.

Run with:

    .venv\\Scripts\\python.exe scripts\\debug_home_page.py

Close the window to exit.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import sys
from datetime import UTC, datetime

from PySide6.QtWidgets import QApplication, QMainWindow

from cranioz.application.project.in_memory_project_repository import (
    InMemoryProjectRepository,
)
from cranioz.application.project.project_catalog import ProjectCatalog
from cranioz.domain.project.project import Project
from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId
from cranioz.domain.project.project_metadata import ProjectMetadata
from cranioz.domain.project.project_status import ProjectStatus
from cranioz.ui.home.home_page import HomePage

# ----- Seed data ----------------------------------------------------

# name, patient_id, created, updated
_FAKE_PROJECTS = [
    ("Gabriel Silva", "12652", "22/06/2025", "22/06/2026"),
    ("Camila Souza", "52269", "02/10/2025", "02/10/2026"),
    ("Lucas Ferreira", "25892", "22/07/2026", "22/07/2026"),
    ("Beatriz Oliveira", "11286", "15/07/2026", "15/07/2026"),
    ("Rafael Santos", "21358", "14/04/2025", "14/10/2025"),
]


def _parse_br_date(value: str) -> datetime:
    """Parse 'DD/MM/YYYY' as a UTC datetime."""
    return datetime.strptime(value, "%d/%m/%Y").replace(tzinfo=UTC)


def _seed_projects(repository: InMemoryProjectRepository) -> None:
    """Create 5 fake projects with fixed timestamps."""
    for name, patient_id, created, updated in _FAKE_PROJECTS:
        pid = ProjectId(patient_id)
        project = Project(
            project_id=pid,
            metadata=ProjectMetadata(
                project_id=pid,
                project_name=f"Caso {name}",
                patient_id=patient_id,
                patient_display_name=name,
                clinical_procedure="orthognathic",
                status=ProjectStatus.ACTIVE,
                created_at=_parse_br_date(created),
                updated_at=_parse_br_date(updated),
                last_opened_at=None,
                schema_version=1,
            ),
        )
        repository.create(project)


def _flow_list(page: HomePage) -> None:
    """Populate the FlowList with three fake flows."""
    page._flow_list_widget().set_flows(
        [
            ("orthognathic_basic", "Cirurgia Ortognática"),
            ("orthognathic_advanced", "Cirurgia Ortognática Avançada"),
            ("facial_implants", "Implantes Faciais"),
        ]
    )


def _connect_debug_signals(page: HomePage) -> None:
    """Connect every HomePage signal to a print() for debugging."""

    def _log(signal_name: str):
        def _handler(*args: object) -> None:
            print(f"[signal] {signal_name} -> {args}")

        return _handler

    page.help_requested.connect(_log("help_requested"))
    page.settings_requested.connect(_log("settings_requested"))
    page.community_requested.connect(_log("community_requested"))
    page.library_requested.connect(_log("library_requested"))
    page.news_requested.connect(_log("news_requested"))
    page.feedback_requested.connect(_log("feedback_requested"))
    page.contribute_requested.connect(_log("contribute_requested"))
    page.new_project_requested.connect(_log("new_project_requested"))
    page.manage_projects_requested.connect(_log("manage_projects_requested"))
    page.project_selected.connect(_log("project_selected"))
    page.flow_selected.connect(_log("flow_selected"))
    page.manage_flows_requested.connect(_log("manage_flows_requested"))
    page.project_open_requested.connect(_log("project_open_requested"))
    page._top_bar_widget().toggle_sidebar_requested.connect(
        lambda: print(
            f"[toggle] sidebar hidden={page._sidebar_widget().isHidden()}"
        )
    )


# ----- Main ---------------------------------------------------------


def main() -> int:
    app = QApplication.instance() or QApplication([])
    app.setStyle("Fusion")

    repository = InMemoryProjectRepository()
    _seed_projects(repository)
    catalog = ProjectCatalog(repository=repository)

    page = HomePage(catalog=catalog)
    _flow_list(page)
    _connect_debug_signals(page)

    window = QMainWindow()
    window.setWindowTitle("CranioZ — Home Page debug")
    window.setCentralWidget(page)
    window.resize(1280, 800)
    window.show()

    return app.exec()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

FILE: scripts/debug_main_window.py

"""Debug script: launch the MainWindow with fake data."""

from __future__ import annotations

import sys
from datetime import UTC, datetime

from PySide6.QtWidgets import QApplication

from cranioz.application.event_bus import EventBus
from cranioz.application.project.in_memory_project_repository import (
    InMemoryProjectRepository,
)
from cranioz.application.project.project_access_policy import (
    AllowAllProjectAccessPolicy,
)
from cranioz.application.project.project_catalog import ProjectCatalog
from cranioz.application.project.project_manager import ProjectManager
from cranioz.domain.project.project import Project
from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId
from cranioz.domain.project.project_metadata import ProjectMetadata
from cranioz.domain.project.project_status import ProjectStatus
from cranioz.ui.editors.editor_registry import EditorRegistry
from cranioz.ui.main_window import MainWindow
from cranioz.ui.theme.service import ThemeName, ThemeService
from cranioz.ui.tools.builtin.log_tool import LogTool
from cranioz.ui.tools.builtin.toggle_tool import ToggleTool
from cranioz.ui.tools.registry.tool_registry import ToolRegistry

_FAKE_PROJECTS = [
    ("Gabriel Silva", "12652", "22/06/2025", "22/06/2026"),
    ("Camila Souza", "52269", "02/10/2025", "02/10/2026"),
    ("Lucas Ferreira", "25892", "22/07/2026", "22/07/2026"),
    ("Beatriz Oliveira", "11286", "15/07/2026", "15/07/2026"),
    ("Rafael Santos", "21358", "14/04/2025", "14/10/2025"),
]


def _parse_br_date(value: str) -> datetime:
    return datetime.strptime(value, "%d/%m/%Y").replace(tzinfo=UTC)


def _seed_projects(repository: InMemoryProjectRepository) -> None:
    for name, patient_id, created, updated in _FAKE_PROJECTS:
        pid = ProjectId(patient_id)
        project = Project(
            project_id=pid,
            metadata=ProjectMetadata(
                project_id=pid,
                project_name=f"Caso {name}",
                patient_id=patient_id,
                patient_display_name=name,
                clinical_procedure="orthognathic",
                status=ProjectStatus.ACTIVE,
                created_at=_parse_br_date(created),
                updated_at=_parse_br_date(updated),
                last_opened_at=None,
                schema_version=1,
            ),
        )
        repository.create(project)


def main() -> int:
    app = QApplication.instance() or QApplication([])
    app.setStyle("Fusion")

    repository = InMemoryProjectRepository()
    _seed_projects(repository)
    catalog = ProjectCatalog(repository=repository)
    manager = ProjectManager(
        repository=repository,
        event_bus=EventBus(),
        access_policy=AllowAllProjectAccessPolicy(),
    )

    editor_registry = EditorRegistry()
    tool_registry = ToolRegistry()
    tool_registry.register("tool.log", LogTool)
    tool_registry.register("tool.toggle", ToggleTool)
    theme_service = ThemeService(theme=ThemeName.DARK)

    window = MainWindow(
        catalog=catalog,
        manager=manager,
        actor="debug",
        editor_registry=editor_registry,
        tool_registry=tool_registry,
        theme_service=theme_service,
    )

    window.home()._flow_list_widget().set_flows(
        [
            ("orthognathic_basic", "Cirurgia Ortognática"),
            ("orthognathic_advanced", "Cirurgia Ortognática Avançada"),
            ("facial_implants", "Implantes Faciais"),
        ]
    )

    # ----- Diagnostics (temporary) -----
    window.home().project_selected.connect(
        lambda pid: print(
            f"[selected] project={pid!r} current={window.home().selected_project_id()!r}"
        )
    )
    window.home().flow_selected.connect(
        lambda fid: print(
            f"[selected] flow={fid!r} current={window.home().selected_flow_id()!r}"
        )
    )

    _orig_set_ready = window.home()._start_button_widget().set_ready

    def _traced_set_ready(ready: bool) -> None:
        print(f"[start_button] set_ready({ready})")
        _orig_set_ready(ready)

    window.home()._start_button_widget().set_ready = _traced_set_ready

    window.show()
    return app.exec()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())


======================================================================
FILE: scripts/debug_workspace.py
======================================================================

"""Debug script: launch the Workspace with a fake ModuleUISpec.

This script is a development tool. It is NOT part of production.

It builds a Workspace with:

- A fake EditorRegistry populated with DummyEditor instances.
- A ToolRegistry with LogTool and ToggleTool.
- A ThemeService (dark).
- A ModuleUISpec that mirrors a typical clinical layout:
    LEFT     -> object_list
    CENTRAL  -> viewport_3d | (mpr_axial / mpr_sagittal)
    RIGHT    -> properties
    BOTTOM   -> console

The Workspace is created with ``debug_colors=True`` so that each
Area has a soft background color that makes its boundary visible:

    LEFT     -> soft blue
    CENTRAL  -> soft green
    RIGHT    -> soft yellow
    BOTTOM   -> soft lavender

The TopOverlay is registered automatically by the Workspace and
will be positioned over the Central Region. During debug it is
painted red so it becomes visible.

Run with:

    .venv\\Scripts\\python.exe scripts\\debug_workspace.py

Close the window to exit.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import sys

from PySide6.QtWidgets import QApplication, QMainWindow

from cranioz.ui.editors.builtin.test_editor import DummyEditor
from cranioz.ui.editors.editor_registry import EditorRegistry
from cranioz.ui.theme.service import ThemeName, ThemeService
from cranioz.ui.tools.builtin.log_tool import LogTool
from cranioz.ui.tools.builtin.toggle_tool import ToggleTool
from cranioz.ui.tools.registry.tool_registry import ToolRegistry
from cranioz.ui.workspace.areas.placement import Placement
from cranioz.ui.workspace.specs.area_spec import AreaSpec
from cranioz.ui.workspace.specs.editor_layout_spec import (
    EditorLayoutSpec,
    SplitDirection,
)
from cranioz.ui.workspace.specs.module_ui_spec import ModuleUISpec
from cranioz.ui.workspace.workspace import Workspace

# ----- Editor registry ----------------------------------------------


def build_editor_registry() -> EditorRegistry:
    """Register DummyEditor under several editor_ids.

    A single DummyEditor class is registered under several ids so
    that the LayoutBuilder can create independent instances for
    each slot. This mirrors the future reality where several
    concrete Editor classes will be registered under distinct ids.
    """
    registry = EditorRegistry()
    for editor_id in (
        "editor.viewport_3d",
        "editor.mpr_axial",
        "editor.mpr_sagittal",
        "editor.object_list",
        "editor.properties",
        "editor.console",
    ):
        registry.register(editor_id, DummyEditor)
    return registry


# ----- Tool registry ------------------------------------------------


def build_tool_registry() -> ToolRegistry:
    registry = ToolRegistry()
    registry.register("tool.log", LogTool)
    registry.register("tool.toggle", ToggleTool)
    return registry


# ----- ModuleUISpec -------------------------------------------------


def build_spec() -> ModuleUISpec:
    """Build a ModuleUISpec that mirrors the prototype layout."""
    return ModuleUISpec(
        areas=(
            AreaSpec(
                area_id="area.left",
                title="Lists",
                placement=Placement.LEFT,
                editor_layout=EditorLayoutSpec.leaf("editor.object_list"),
            ),
            AreaSpec(
                area_id="area.central",
                title="Editing",
                placement=Placement.CENTRAL,
                editor_layout=EditorLayoutSpec.split(
                    SplitDirection.HORIZONTAL,
                    (
                        EditorLayoutSpec.leaf("editor.viewport_3d"),
                        EditorLayoutSpec.split(
                            SplitDirection.VERTICAL,
                            (
                                EditorLayoutSpec.leaf("editor.mpr_axial"),
                                EditorLayoutSpec.leaf("editor.mpr_sagittal"),
                            ),
                        ),
                    ),
                ),
            ),
            AreaSpec(
                area_id="area.right",
                title="Tools",
                placement=Placement.RIGHT,
                editor_layout=EditorLayoutSpec.leaf("editor.properties"),
            ),
            AreaSpec(
                area_id="area.bottom",
                title="Console",
                placement=Placement.BOTTOM,
                editor_layout=EditorLayoutSpec.leaf("editor.console"),
            ),
        ),
    )


# ----- Main ---------------------------------------------------------


def main() -> int:
    app = QApplication.instance() or QApplication([])

    app.setStyle("Fusion")

    workspace = Workspace(
        editor_registry=build_editor_registry(),
        tool_registry=build_tool_registry(),
        theme_service=ThemeService(theme=ThemeName.DARK),
        debug_colors=True,
    )
    workspace.load_spec(build_spec())

    window = QMainWindow()
    window.setWindowTitle("CranioZ — Workspace debug")
    window.setCentralWidget(workspace)
    window.resize(1280, 800)
    window.show()

    return app.exec()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

"""CranioZ — virtual surgical planning for cranio-maxillofacial surgery.

This package exposes the public API of the CranioZ platform.
Internal modules should not be imported directly; use the entry points
and the public API defined here.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from importlib.metadata import PackageNotFoundError, version

try:
    __version__ = version("cranioz")
except PackageNotFoundError:  # running from source without installation
    __version__ = "0.0.0+local"

__author__ = "Jozeias Fernandes de Sousa"
__license__ = "AGPL-3.0-or-later"

__all__ = [
    "__author__",
    "__license__",
    "__version__",
]



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/__main__.py
======================================================================

"""CranioZ application entry point.

Boot sequence:
1. Load configuration.
2. Configure logging.
3. Initialize the project path resolver.
4. Initialize the event bus.
5. Start the Qt application loop.

The main window is not created in this phase.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
import logging.config
from pathlib import Path

import yaml
from PySide6.QtWidgets import QApplication

from cranioz import __version__
from cranioz.application.event_bus import EventBus
from cranioz.config.loader import ConfigurationLoader
from cranioz.config.schema import Config
from cranioz.infrastructure.app.app_path_resolver import AppPathResolver

_logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

_DEFAULT_LOGGING_CONFIG = Path(__file__).parent / "config" / "defaults" / "logging.yaml"


def _configure_logging(config: Config) -> None:
    """Configure logging from the packaged YAML, then apply config overrides."""
    if _DEFAULT_LOGGING_CONFIG.exists():
        with _DEFAULT_LOGGING_CONFIG.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
            logging.config.dictConfig(yaml.safe_load(fh))
    logging.getLogger().setLevel(config.logging.level)


def main() -> int:
    """Run the CranioZ application."""
    config = ConfigurationLoader().load()
    _configure_logging(config)
    _logger.info("CranioZ %s starting", __version__)

    app_paths = AppPathResolver()
    app_paths.initialize(config.paths)
    _logger.info("Projects root: %s", app_paths.projects_root)

    event_bus = EventBus()
    _logger.debug("Event bus initialized: %r", event_bus)

    app = QApplication.instance() or QApplication([])
    _logger.info("Boot complete")
    return app.exec()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/application/event_bus.py
======================================================================

"""Application event bus.

Minimal synchronous publish/subscribe bus for application-level events.
Threading and async dispatch are out of scope for this phase.

Rules:
- Subscribers receive events synchronously, in registration order.
- Publishing an event with no subscribers is a no-op.
- A failing subscriber does not prevent other subscribers from running;
  errors are collected and re-raised after all subscribers have been
  notified.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections import defaultdict
from collections.abc import Callable
from typing import Any


class EventBusError(Exception):
    """Raised when subscriber dispatch fails."""


EventHandler = Callable[[Any], None]


class EventBus:
    """Synchronous in-process event bus."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._subscribers: dict[type, list[EventHandler]] = defaultdict(list)

    def subscribe(self, event_type: type, handler: EventHandler) -> None:
        """Register a handler for a specific event type."""
        self._subscribers[event_type].append(handler)

    def unsubscribe(self, event_type: type, handler: EventHandler) -> None:
        """Remove a previously registered handler.

        No-op if the handler is not registered.
        """
        handlers = self._subscribers.get(event_type)
        if not handlers:
            return
        try:
            handlers.remove(handler)
        except ValueError:
            return

    def publish(self, event: Any) -> None:
        """Publish an event to all handlers registered for its type.

        Dispatches in registration order. If any handler raises, the
        remaining handlers still run, and the collected exceptions are
        re-raised as an EventBusError at the end.
        """
        handlers = list(self._subscribers.get(type(event), ()))
        errors: list[BaseException] = []
        for handler in handlers:
            try:
                handler(event)
            except Exception as exc:
                errors.append(exc)
        if errors:
            raise EventBusError(
                f"{len(errors)} subscriber(s) failed while publishing {type(event).__name__}"
            ) from errors[0]

    def subscriber_count(self, event_type: type) -> int:
        """Return the number of handlers registered for an event type."""
        return len(self._subscribers.get(event_type, ()))

"""Project subsystem — application layer.

Public API of the Project management subsystem:

- `ProjectRepository` — persistence port (Protocol).
- `InMemoryProjectRepository` — reference implementation, test-only.
- The `ProjectRepositoryError` family — application-level errors.

Infrastructure adapters (e.g. `FilesystemProjectRepository`) live in
`cranioz.infrastructure.project` and are imported by the bootstrap,
not by this package.

from cranioz.application.project.in_memory_project_repository import (

)
from cranioz.application.project.project_errors import (
    ProjectAlreadyExists,
    ProjectCorrupted,
    ProjectError,
    ProjectNotFound,
    ProjectRepositoryError,
    ProjectStorageError,

__all__ = [

======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/application/project/in_memory_project_repository.py
======================================================================

"""InMemoryProjectRepository — reference implementation of ProjectRepository.

This implementation is the canonical reference for the contract defined
in `project_repository.py`. It is used by:

- Contract tests, which run the same suite against every implementation.
- Higher-layer tests (Project Manager, Project Catalog), where filesystem
  I/O would make tests slow and brittle.
- Early integration of the Home Page, before the filesystem adapter is
  available.

It is not a toy. It respects every documented failure mode of the
Protocol, including `ProjectAlreadyExists`, `ProjectNotFound`,
`ProjectCorrupted` (for previously-injected corrupted entries), and
`ProjectStorageError` (for explicitly-injected failure modes).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from cranioz.application.project.project_errors import (
    ProjectAlreadyExists,
    ProjectCorrupted,
    ProjectNotFound,
)
from cranioz.domain.project.project import Project
from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId
from cranioz.domain.project.project_summary import ProjectSummary


class InMemoryProjectRepository:
    """Reference implementation of `ProjectRepository` backed by a dict.

    Not thread-safe. The Project Manager (Peça 7.5) is the only
    caller, and the application runs it on the main thread.
    """

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        # Stores the full aggregate. Values are the *same* instances
        # the caller passed in `create` or `update`. This mirrors the
        # semantics of a filesystem repository: persistence does not
        # deep-copy, it stores the serialized representation; callers
        # that mutate the Project in place are responsible for calling
        # `update` to persist the mutation. Tests that need defensive
        # copying should copy explicitly.
        self._projects: dict[ProjectId, Project] = {}

        # Set of ids that should be reported as corrupted on `get`.
        # Used by tests to exercise the `ProjectCorrupted` path
        # without simulating a filesystem. Never populated by the
        # normal `create`/`update` path.
        self._corrupted: set[ProjectId] = set()

    # ----- Test support ---------------------------------------------

    def _mark_corrupted(self, project_id: ProjectId) -> None:
        """Mark a project as corrupted for testing purposes.

        After this call, `get` raises `ProjectCorrupted` for the given
        id, and `list_metadata` skips it. Used by contract tests to
        verify that a `ProjectCorrupted` does not crash `list_metadata`.
        """
        self._corrupted.add(project_id)

    # ----- ProjectRepository ----------------------------------------

    def create(self, project: Project) -> None:
        if project.project_id in self._projects:
            raise ProjectAlreadyExists(
                f"project already exists: {project.project_id.value!r}",
                project_id=project.project_id,
            )
        self._projects[project.project_id] = project

    def get(self, project_id: ProjectId) -> Project:
        if project_id in self._corrupted:
            raise ProjectCorrupted(
                f"project is corrupted: {project_id.value!r}",
                project_id=project_id,
            )
        try:
            return self._projects[project_id]
        except KeyError as exc:
            raise ProjectNotFound(
                f"project not found: {project_id.value!r}",
                project_id=project_id,
            ) from exc

    def update(self, project: Project) -> None:
        if project.project_id not in self._projects:
            raise ProjectNotFound(
                f"project not found: {project.project_id.value!r}",
                project_id=project.project_id,
            )
        self._projects[project.project_id] = project

    def delete(self, project_id: ProjectId) -> None:
        if project_id not in self._projects:
            raise ProjectNotFound(
                f"project not found: {project_id.value!r}",
                project_id=project_id,
            )
        del self._projects[project_id]
        # Corrupted markers become irrelevant once the id is deleted.
        self._corrupted.discard(project_id)

    def exists(self, project_id: ProjectId) -> bool:
        return project_id in self._projects

    def list_metadata(self) -> tuple[ProjectSummary, ...]:
        summaries: list[ProjectSummary] = []
        for project_id, project in self._projects.items():
            if project_id in self._corrupted:
                continue
            summaries.append(ProjectSummary.from_metadata(project.metadata))
        return tuple(summaries)

    def list_ids(self) -> tuple[ProjectId, ...]:
        return tuple(self._projects.keys())



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/application/project/project_access_policy.py
======================================================================

"""ProjectAccessPolicy — authorization contract for project lifecycle.

The policy answers "may this actor perform this operation on this
project?". It is evaluated at the Project Manager boundary
(project.md §15): the Manager consults the policy before mutating
state, never after.

Design rules (see `project.md` §15, §28):

- The policy is a Protocol. Concrete implementations may be
  permissive (single-user, development, first boot) or restrictive
  (institutional deployments, role-based access).
- The policy is **read-only**. It does not mutate state, does not
  publish events, does not access persistence. It receives an
  `Actor` and a `ProjectId` (or nothing, for `can_create`) and
  returns a boolean.
- An `Actor` is a string for now. It identifies who is performing
  the operation. In single-user mode (the initial deployment), the
  bootstrap passes a stable identifier (e.g. the OS username, or a
  configured professional id). When the application gains a proper
  user model, `Actor` will become a richer value object; the
  Protocol signature will not need to change for the common case.
- `AllowAllProjectAccessPolicy` is the initial implementation. It
  returns True for every operation. It exists so the Project
  Manager has a policy to consult from day one, and so tests can
  substitute a denying policy to verify the Manager's error paths.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol, runtime_checkable

from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId

# An Actor identifies who is performing the operation. Kept as a
# string alias for now; the application has no user model yet.
# When a proper user model exists (roles, institutions), this alias
# becomes a class, and callers that already pass strings continue
# to work via a compatibility shim.
Actor = str


@runtime_checkable
class ProjectAccessPolicy(Protocol):
    """Authorization contract for project lifecycle operations."""

    def can_create(self, actor: Actor) -> bool:
        """Return True if `actor` may create a new project."""
        ...

    def can_open(self, actor: Actor, project_id: ProjectId) -> bool:
        """Return True if `actor` may open the given project."""
        ...

    def can_update(self, actor: Actor, project_id: ProjectId) -> bool:
        """Return True if `actor` may update the given project."""
        ...

    def can_delete(self, actor: Actor, project_id: ProjectId) -> bool:
        """Return True if `actor` may delete the given project."""
        ...


class AllowAllProjectAccessPolicy:
    """Permissive policy: every actor may do everything.

    This is the initial implementation for single-user deployments
    and for tests that do not care about authorization. It is a
    deliberate no-op, not a security boundary. A future
    RoleBasedProjectAccessPolicy will replace it for institutional
    deployments without changing the Project Manager's code.
    """

    def can_create(self, actor: Actor) -> bool:
        return True

    def can_open(self, actor: Actor, project_id: ProjectId) -> bool:
        return True

    def can_update(self, actor: Actor, project_id: ProjectId) -> bool:
        return True

    def can_delete(self, actor: Actor, project_id: ProjectId) -> bool:
        return True



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/application/project/project_catalog.py
======================================================================

"""ProjectCatalog — read-only discovery projection.

The ProjectCatalog is the read model consumed by the Home Page
(project.md §5, §7; startup_project_opening_workflow.md §4). It answers
"which projects exist and what do they look like?" without loading
clinical data.

Design rules (see `project.md`):

- The catalog is **read-only**. It does not create, modify, delete,
  archive, or restore projects.
- The catalog is **not the source of truth**. It is a projection of
  what `ProjectRepository.list_metadata()` reports. If the repository
  changes, the next call to the catalog reflects the change; there is
  no cache to invalidate.
- The catalog **does not implement lifecycle policy**. It reports
  what exists. Whether a project is "openable", "in use", or
  "archived per policy" is the Project Manager's concern (piece 7.5).
- The catalog **does not maintain an independent authoritative list**.
  It has no `_projects` attribute. Every call delegates to the
  repository.
- The catalog **does not read from the filesystem or any other I/O**.
  It only consumes the repository, which already translates
  infrastructure failures into the `ProjectRepositoryError` family.

Why no cache in this piece:

The catalog will be called every time the Home Page refreshes. On a
local SSD, reading a few `project.json` files takes microseconds per
project. Premature caching would introduce invalidation bugs (stale
data after `ProjectDeleted`, cache misses during module switch) for
no measured benefit. If future profiling shows the catalog is a hot
path, a cache will be added in a separate piece, explicitly
invalidated by subscribing to `ProjectCreated` / `ProjectUpdated` /
`ProjectDeleted`. Until then: no cache.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from cranioz.application.project.project_repository import ProjectRepository
from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId
from cranioz.domain.project.project_summary import ProjectSummary


class ProjectCatalog:
    """Read-only projection of the projects known to a repository.

    The catalog is the entry point for discovery: listing, searching,
    and looking up project summaries. It is not the entry point for
    lifecycle operations (create/open/close/delete); those belong to
    the Project Manager (piece 7.5).
    """

    def __init__(self, repository: ProjectRepository) -> None:
        self._repository = repository

    # ----- Listing ---------------------------------------------------

    def list_projects(self) -> tuple[ProjectSummary, ...]:
        """Return summaries of all projects known to the repository.

        Order is unspecified. Returns an empty tuple when no project
        exists (not an error). Propagates `ProjectStorageError` if the
        repository cannot list at all.
        """
        return self._repository.list_metadata()

    # ----- Lookup ----------------------------------------------------

    def get_project_summary(
        self,
        project_id: ProjectId,
    ) -> ProjectSummary | None:
        """Return the summary of a specific project, or None if unknown.

        The catalog does **not** raise `ProjectNotFound`. It returns
        `None`. Rationale: for discovery purposes, "not in the catalog"
        and "not persisted" are equivalent — the Home Page shows the
        same empty state either way. Callers that need to distinguish
        "not found" from "storage failure" should query the repository
        directly, or use `ProjectManager.get_project` (piece 7.5),
        which does raise.

        Propagates `ProjectStorageError` if the repository cannot list
        at all (as opposed to "the specific id is not present").
        """
        for summary in self._repository.list_metadata():
            if summary.project_id == project_id:
                return summary
        return None

    # ----- Search ----------------------------------------------------

    def search_projects(self, query: str) -> tuple[ProjectSummary, ...]:
        """Return summaries whose text fields match `query`.

        Matching is **case-insensitive substring** over:
        - `project_name`
        - `patient_display_name`

        No regex. No fuzzy matching. No ranking. `query` is treated as
        a literal substring, after `strip()`. An empty or
        whitespace-only query returns all projects (equivalent to
        `list_projects()`), which is the natural behavior for a
        search-as-you-type input.

        Rationale for no regex: the input comes from a user-facing
        search box. Allowing regex opens a ReDoS surface and is not
        what users expect from a "search" field. If advanced matching
        is ever needed, it will be an explicit `advanced_search`
        method with its own contract.

        Order is unspecified. Returns an empty tuple when nothing
        matches.
        """
        normalized = query.strip().lower()
        all_summaries = self._repository.list_metadata()
        if not normalized:
            return all_summaries

        matches: list[ProjectSummary] = []
        for summary in all_summaries:
            if (
                normalized in summary.project_name.lower()
                or normalized in summary.patient_display_name.lower()
            ):
                matches.append(summary)
        return tuple(matches)

    # ----- Convenience -----------------------------------------------

    def count(self) -> int:
        """Return the number of projects known to the repository."""
        return len(self._repository.list_metadata())

    def is_empty(self) -> bool:
        """Return True if the repository reports no projects."""
        return len(self._repository.list_metadata()) == 0

    def contains(self, project_id: ProjectId) -> bool:
        """Return True if a project with the given id is discoverable.

        Cheap check; implemented via `get_project_summary`.
        """
        return self.get_project_summary(project_id) is not None



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/application/project/project_context.py
======================================================================

"""ProjectContext — immutable initialization snapshot.

The ProjectContext is created when the Project Manager opens a
project (Peça 7.5). It is the runtime representation handed to the
Workspace: everything the Workspace needs to know about the active
project, and nothing more.

Design rules (see `project.md` §17, `startup_project_opening_workflow.md` §9):

- The context is **frozen**. The Workspace must never mutate it.
- The context is **not** the Project. It does not own the project's
  lifecycle; the Project Manager does. Discarding the context does not
  discard the project.
- The context carries **no service locator**. Services (ProjectManager,
  Repository, EventBus, ...) are injected via dependency injection by
  the bootstrap, not resolved through the context. The note in
  `project.md` §17 is explicit: the context is data, not a registry.
- The context carries **no physical paths**. Logical references only
  (`project_id`, `workflow_id`); physical path resolution belongs to
  `ProjectPathResolver` at the infrastructure boundary.
- `workflow_id` and `workflow_config` are optional. A project may be
  opened before a clinical workflow is selected
  (`startup_project_opening_workflow.md` §10: "If no flow is resolved,
  the Workspace opens in flow selection mode"). The two are either
  both set or both `None` — a workflow id without a config is
  meaningless.
- `object_references` is a tuple, not a list. Immutability is
  structural, not just by convention.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any

from cranioz.domain.patient.patient_summary import PatientSummary
from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId
from cranioz.domain.project.project_metadata import ProjectMetadata


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ObjectReference:
    """A logical reference to a domain object visible to the Workspace.

    The context does not carry the object itself; it carries enough
    to *identify* it. Resolution to the live object happens through
    the Project's `DomainObjectStore` (see `scene.md` §4), which is
    not part of the context.
    """

    object_id: str
    kind: str
    display_name: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.object_id:
            raise ValueError("object_id must not be empty")
        if not self.kind:
            raise ValueError("kind must not be empty")
        if not self.display_name:
            raise ValueError("display_name must not be empty")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProjectContext:
    """Immutable snapshot handed to the Workspace when a project opens.

    Every field is a plain domain value. No service, no callable, no
    physical path. The Workspace consumes it; it does not mutate it and
    it does not fetch anything the context does not carry.
    """

    project_id: ProjectId
    metadata: ProjectMetadata
    patient_summary: PatientSummary
    workflow_id: str | None = None
    workflow_config: dict[str, Any] | None = None
    object_references: tuple[ObjectReference, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.metadata.project_id != self.project_id:
            raise ValueError(
                "metadata.project_id must match project_id "
                f"(metadata={self.metadata.project_id.value!r}, "
                f"context={self.project_id.value!r})"
            )

        if (self.workflow_id is None) != (self.workflow_config is None):
            raise ValueError(
                "workflow_id and workflow_config must be set together or both be None"
            )

    def has_workflow(self) -> bool:
        """Return True if a clinical workflow has been selected."""
        return self.workflow_id is not None

    def with_workflow(
        self,
        workflow_id: str,
        workflow_config: dict[str, Any],
    ) -> ProjectContext:
        """Return a new context with the workflow attached."""
        if not workflow_id:
            raise ValueError("workflow_id must not be empty")
        return ProjectContext(
            project_id=self.project_id,
            metadata=self.metadata,
            patient_summary=self.patient_summary,
            workflow_id=workflow_id,
            workflow_config=dict(workflow_config),
            object_references=self.object_references,
        )

    def with_object_references(
        self,
        object_references: tuple[ObjectReference, ...],
    ) -> ProjectContext:
        """Return a new context with different object references."""
        return ProjectContext(
            project_id=self.project_id,
            metadata=self.metadata,
            patient_summary=self.patient_summary,
            workflow_id=self.workflow_id,
            workflow_config=dict(self.workflow_config) if self.workflow_config else None,
            object_references=object_references,
        )



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/application/project/project_errors.py
======================================================================

"""Application-level errors for the Project subsystem.

These errors belong to the Application layer. They are the vocabulary
through which the Project Manager and the UI communicate about project
lifecycle failures. They are intentionally independent of any
infrastructure concept: an `OSError`, a `FileNotFoundError`, or a
JSON `DecodeError` must be translated into one of these errors at the
repository boundary, never propagated as-is.

Design rules (see `project.md` §27):

- Every error derives from `ProjectError`, so callers can catch the
  whole family with one `except`.
- Errors carry enough context to be actionable: the `project_id` that
  failed, and (when known) the underlying cause.
- Errors are not `ValueError` or `KeyError` subclasses. They are a
  separate hierarchy so that callers do not accidentally conflate a
  domain validation error with an application lifecycle error.

Two families live under `ProjectError`:

- `ProjectRepositoryError` and subclasses — persistence failures.
  Raised by repository implementations (filesystem, in-memory).
- `ProjectAccessDenied`, `ProjectInUse` — application decisions.
  Raised by the Project Manager before touching persistence. They are
  not persistence errors and must not be caught as such.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId


class ProjectError(Exception):
    """Base of all application-level Project subsystem errors.

    Catching `ProjectError` catches every failure the Project Manager
    can raise. Catching a more specific subclass lets the caller react
    differently to, e.g., "not found" vs "already exists".
    """


# ----- Persistence failures -----------------------------------------


class ProjectRepositoryError(ProjectError):
    """Base of all errors raised by a `ProjectRepository` implementation.

    Repositories raise this family of errors when a persistence operation
    fails for reasons that the repository itself cannot resolve. The
    Project Manager (which coordinates the lifecycle) is responsible for
    translating these into user-facing decisions.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        message: str,
        *,
        project_id: ProjectId | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        self.project_id = project_id
        super().__init__(message)


class ProjectNotFound(ProjectRepositoryError):
    """Raised when a requested project does not exist.

    Used by `get` and (with a different policy) by `update` and `delete`.
    The message includes the `project_id` so that logs are diagnosable
    without re-reading the exception attribute.
    """


class ProjectAlreadyExists(ProjectRepositoryError):
    """Raised when `create` is called with an id that already exists.

    Projects are identified by a stable `ProjectId`; creating a second
    project with the same id is a programming error at the application
    level, never a silent overwrite.
    """


class ProjectCorrupted(ProjectRepositoryError):
    """Raised when a project's persisted representation is unreadable.

    Examples: a malformed `project.json`, a missing required file, a
    schema version that cannot be migrated, a conflicting metadata
    file. The repository does not attempt recovery; it reports the
    problem so that the Project Manager can apply the recovery policy.
    """


class ProjectStorageError(ProjectRepositoryError):
    """Raised when the underlying storage rejects an operation.

    Examples: permission denied, disk full, a file locked by another
    process, an I/O error on the network share. The repository wraps
    the underlying `OSError` as `__cause__`; callers that need the
    original exception can inspect `__cause__` explicitly.
    """


# ----- Application decisions ---------------------------------------


class ProjectAccessDenied(ProjectError):
    """Raised when the access policy denies a project operation.

    The operation was not attempted: no state was mutated, no event
    was published. The policy is consulted before any other step, so
    this error means "you were not allowed to ask", not "your request
    was invalid".
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        message: str,
        *,
        project_id: ProjectId | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        self.project_id = project_id
        super().__init__(message)


class ProjectInUse(ProjectError):
    """Raised when an operation conflicts with an open project.

    The project is currently open (has an active runtime context);
    the caller must close it first. This is a deliberate refusal,
    not an implicit close: closing and deleting are two distinct
    commands with different effects, and mixing them could discard
    a context the user is still using.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        message: str,
        *,
        project_id: ProjectId | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        self.project_id = project_id
        super().__init__(message)



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/application/project/project_events.py
======================================================================

"""Project lifecycle events.

These events communicate state changes that have *already happened*.
They are published by the Project Manager (Peça 7.5) after a successful
repository operation, via the EventBus. The events themselves are pure
data; this module does not publish, subscribe, or route anything.

Design rules (see `project.md` §22, `overview.md` §13):

- Events are **facts**, not intents. "ProjectOpened" means the project
  is open. If a caller sees this event, it does not need to check.
- Events are **frozen**. Observers must not mutate them.
- Events are **not commands**. The fact that a project was deleted does
  not mean an observer may now act; that is a separate concern.
- Every event carries the `project_id` it refers to. This is the
  minimum contract every observer relies on.
- Events carry **payload specific to their lifecycle moment**:
  `ProjectCreated` carries the initial metadata; `ProjectUpdated` carries
  the new metadata; `ProjectOpened` carries the `ProjectContext`
  (the Workspace needs it); `ProjectClosed` carries nothing extra (the
  context is discarded by the Workspace, not carried in the event).

The event bus is not imported here. The publication mechanism is the
Project Manager's concern.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from cranioz.application.project.project_context import ProjectContext
from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId
from cranioz.domain.project.project_metadata import ProjectMetadata


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProjectCreated:
    """A new project has been persisted.

    Carries the metadata with which the project was created. Observers
    (e.g. the Project Catalog, Peça 7.4) can update their read model
    without re-fetching from the repository.
    """

    project_id: ProjectId
    metadata: ProjectMetadata


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProjectOpened:
    """A project has been opened and a runtime context created.

    Carries the `ProjectContext` that the Workspace will consume. The
    event itself is published *after* the context is built, so
    observers can rely on its completeness.
    """

    project_id: ProjectId
    context: ProjectContext


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProjectUpdated:
    """A project's metadata or state has changed.

    Carries the *new* metadata. The previous metadata is not carried;
    observers that need the previous value must have cached it, which
    is not a supported pattern. Observers see the current state.
    """

    project_id: ProjectId
    metadata: ProjectMetadata


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProjectClosing:
    """A project is about to close, but has not closed yet.

    Published **before** pending state is persisted and before the
    context is discarded. Observers that need to react while the
    context is still active (e.g. autosave flush) subscribe to this
    event, not `ProjectClosed`.
    """

    project_id: ProjectId


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProjectClosed:
    """A project has closed and its runtime context is discarded.

    Published **after** the context has been released. Observers that
    held a reference to the context must not use it after this event.
    The project remains persisted and discoverable; only the runtime
    state is gone.
    """

    project_id: ProjectId


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProjectDeleted:
    """A project has been deleted from persistent storage.

    Published **after** the repository confirms the deletion. The
    project is no longer discoverable. Observers (Catalog, Home Page,
    recent-projects list) must remove it from their view.
    """

    project_id: ProjectId



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/application/project/project_manager.py
======================================================================

"""ProjectManager — single authority for project lifecycle.

The Project Manager is the only component allowed to create, open,
update, close, or delete a project (project.md §4, §25). It
coordinates:

- `ProjectRepository` (piece 7.3): persistence.
- `ProjectCatalog` (piece 7.4): not consulted directly. The catalog
  is a pure projection; it reflects the repository on every call.
  The Manager does not need to invalidate or refresh it.
- `EventBus`: lifecycle event publication (piece 7.2 vocabulary).
- `ProjectAccessPolicy`: authorization, evaluated before mutating
  state.

Design rules (see `project.md` §4, §16, §17, §21, §22, §25):

- The Manager is **not thread-safe**. It runs on the application's
  main thread. Concurrency is a separate concern (project.md §26,
  pending ADR); this piece does not introduce locks.
- The Manager does **not** access the filesystem directly. Every
  persistence operation goes through the repository, which
  translates infrastructure failures into the
  `ProjectRepositoryError` family.
- The Manager **does not build ProjectContext from a patient
  registry**. The context's `PatientSummary` is a projection of
  the project's own metadata (`patient_id`, `patient_display_name`);
  `birth_date` and `sex` are left as `None` because the project
  does not carry them. This keeps `open_project` self-contained
  (no dependency on a patient repository), and documents that the
  context is not a clinical record.
- The Manager **publishes events only after a successful
  mutation**. If the repository raises, no event is published.
  This is the transaction boundary (project.md §10, §13).
- `delete_project` on an open project raises `ProjectInUse`
  **before** touching state or publishing events. Closing
  implicitly would mix two distinct commands and could discard a
  context the user is still using. The caller must close first,
  then delete.
- `close_project` on a non-open project is a **silent no-op** (no
  events published). Closing is idempotent; a second close is not
  an error.

Active contexts:

The Manager holds a dict `{ProjectId: ProjectContext}` for the
currently open projects. In this piece, at most one project is
open at a time in practice (the Workspace is single-context), but
the Manager does not enforce that — the enforcement is a
Workspace/Application policy decision (project.md §21). If a
second `open_project` is called while another is open, both remain
open in the Manager; the caller decides which to close. This
matches `project.md` §21 ("Whether another project can be opened
before the current project is closed is an application/session
policy and should not be implemented independently by the Home
Page").
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import UTC, datetime
from typing import Any

from cranioz.application.event_bus import EventBus
from cranioz.application.project.project_access_policy import (
    Actor,
    ProjectAccessPolicy,
)
from cranioz.application.project.project_context import ProjectContext
from cranioz.application.project.project_errors import (
    ProjectAccessDenied,
    ProjectInUse,
)
from cranioz.application.project.project_events import (
    ProjectClosed,
    ProjectClosing,
    ProjectCreated,
    ProjectDeleted,
    ProjectOpened,
    ProjectUpdated,
)
from cranioz.application.project.project_repository import ProjectRepository
from cranioz.domain.patient.patient_summary import PatientSummary
from cranioz.domain.patient.value_objects.patient_id import PatientId
from cranioz.domain.project.project import Project
from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId
from cranioz.domain.project.project_metadata import ProjectMetadata
from cranioz.domain.project.project_status import ProjectStatus


class ProjectManager:
    """Single authority for the project lifecycle."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        repository: ProjectRepository,
        event_bus: EventBus,
        access_policy: ProjectAccessPolicy,
    ) -> None:
        self._repository = repository
        self._event_bus = event_bus
        self._access_policy = access_policy
        self._active_contexts: dict[ProjectId, ProjectContext] = {}

    # ----- Create ----------------------------------------------------

    def create_project(
        self,
        actor: Actor,
        *,
        project_id: ProjectId,
        project_name: str,
        patient_id: PatientId,
        patient_display_name: str,
        clinical_procedure: str,
        status: ProjectStatus = ProjectStatus.DRAFT,
    ) -> Project:
        """Create, persist, and publish a new project.

        Raises:
            ProjectAccessDenied: the policy denies the operation.
            ProjectAlreadyExists: the project id is already persisted.
            ProjectStorageError: the repository cannot persist.
        """
        if not self._access_policy.can_create(actor):
            raise ProjectAccessDenied(
                f"actor {actor!r} is not allowed to create projects"
            )

        now = _now()
        metadata = ProjectMetadata(
            project_id=project_id,
            project_name=project_name,
            patient_id=patient_id.value,
            patient_display_name=patient_display_name,
            clinical_procedure=clinical_procedure,
            status=status,
            created_at=now,
            updated_at=now,
            last_opened_at=None,
            schema_version=1,
        )
        project = Project(project_id=project_id, metadata=metadata)

        self._repository.create(project)
        self._event_bus.publish(
            ProjectCreated(project_id=project.project_id, metadata=metadata)
        )
        return project

    # ----- Open ------------------------------------------------------

    def open_project(
        self,
        actor: Actor,
        project_id: ProjectId,
        *,
        workflow_id: str | None = None,
        workflow_config: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
    ) -> ProjectContext:
        """Load a project, build its context, and publish ProjectOpened.

        If the project is already open, returns the existing context
        unchanged and does not publish a second ProjectOpened.

        Raises:
            ProjectAccessDenied: the policy denies the operation.
            ProjectNotFound: the project is not persisted.
            ProjectCorrupted: the persisted form is unreadable.
            ProjectStorageError: the repository cannot read.
        """
        if not self._access_policy.can_open(actor, project_id):
            raise ProjectAccessDenied(
                f"actor {actor!r} is not allowed to open {project_id.value!r}"
            )

        existing = self._active_contexts.get(project_id)
        if existing is not None:
            return existing

        project = self._repository.get(project_id)

        now = _now()
        updated_metadata = project.metadata.with_last_opened(now=now)
        updated_project = Project(
            project_id=project.project_id,
            metadata=updated_metadata,
        )
        self._repository.update(updated_project)

        context = self._build_context(
            updated_project,
            workflow_id=workflow_id,
            workflow_config=workflow_config,
        )
        self._active_contexts[project_id] = context

        self._event_bus.publish(
            ProjectOpened(project_id=project_id, context=context)
        )
        return context

    # ----- Update ----------------------------------------------------

    def update_project(self, actor: Actor, project: Project) -> None:
        """Persist an updated project and publish ProjectUpdated.

        Raises:
            ProjectAccessDenied: the policy denies the operation.
            ProjectNotFound: the project is not persisted.
            ProjectStorageError: the repository cannot persist.
        """
        if not self._access_policy.can_update(actor, project.project_id):
            raise ProjectAccessDenied(
                f"actor {actor!r} is not allowed to update "
                f"{project.project_id.value!r}"
            )

        self._repository.update(project)
        self._event_bus.publish(
            ProjectUpdated(
                project_id=project.project_id,
                metadata=project.metadata,
            )
        )

    # ----- Close -----------------------------------------------------

    def close_project(self, actor: Actor, project_id: ProjectId) -> None:
        """Close an open project. No-op if the project is not open.

        Publishes ProjectClosing before releasing the context, then
        ProjectClosed after. Does not touch persistence: the project's
        persisted metadata is not modified by close (last_opened_at
        was already updated at open time).

        Close does not need an access check: closing a project you
        opened is always allowed. The actor parameter is accepted for
        symmetry and future auditing.
        """
        del actor  # explicitly unused; close needs no authorization
        if project_id not in self._active_contexts:
            return  # silent no-op; close is idempotent

        self._event_bus.publish(ProjectClosing(project_id=project_id))
        del self._active_contexts[project_id]
        self._event_bus.publish(ProjectClosed(project_id=project_id))

    # ----- Delete ----------------------------------------------------

    def delete_project(self, actor: Actor, project_id: ProjectId) -> None:
        """Delete a persisted project and publish ProjectDeleted.

        Raises:
            ProjectAccessDenied: the policy denies the operation.
            ProjectInUse: the project is currently open. Close it
                first. This is a deliberate refusal: closing
                implicitly would mix two distinct commands and could
                discard a context the user is still using.
            ProjectNotFound: the project is not persisted.
            ProjectStorageError: the repository cannot delete.
        """
        if not self._access_policy.can_delete(actor, project_id):
            raise ProjectAccessDenied(
                f"actor {actor!r} is not allowed to delete "
                f"{project_id.value!r}"
            )

        if project_id in self._active_contexts:
            raise ProjectInUse(
                f"cannot delete open project {project_id.value!r}; "
                f"close it first"
            )

        self._repository.delete(project_id)
        self._event_bus.publish(ProjectDeleted(project_id=project_id))

    # ----- Active context --------------------------------------------

    def get_active_context(
        self,
        project_id: ProjectId,
    ) -> ProjectContext | None:
        """Return the runtime context if the project is open, else None."""
        return self._active_contexts.get(project_id)

    def is_open(self, project_id: ProjectId) -> bool:
        """Return True if the project currently has an active context."""
        return project_id in self._active_contexts

    # ----- Internals -------------------------------------------------

    def _build_context(
        self,
        project: Project,
        *,
        workflow_id: str | None,
        workflow_config: dict[str, Any] | None,
    ) -> ProjectContext:
        """Build the runtime context from the project's own data.

        The PatientSummary carried by the context is a **projection
        of the project metadata**, not a fresh read from a patient
        registry. Fields derived:

        - patient_id           <- metadata.patient_id
        - display_name         <- metadata.patient_display_name
        - birth_date           <- None (not carried by the project)
        - sex                  <- None (not carried by the project)

        Rationale: opening a project must be self-contained.
        Requiring a patient repository at open time would introduce a
        dependency on the patient registry, with availability and
        consistency concerns that belong to a different flow. The
        Workspace consumes the context for the project; if it later
        needs fresh patient data, that is a separate query against a
        dedicated patient provider, not part of `open_project`.
        """
        patient_summary = PatientSummary(
            patient_id=PatientId(project.metadata.patient_id),
            display_name=project.metadata.patient_display_name,
            birth_date=None,
            sex=None,
        )
        return ProjectContext(
            project_id=project.project_id,
            metadata=project.metadata,
            patient_summary=patient_summary,
            workflow_id=workflow_id,
            workflow_config=workflow_config,
        )


# ----- Internals -----------------------------------------------------


def _now() -> datetime:
    """Current UTC time. Isolated so tests can monkeypatch it."""
    return datetime.now(tz=UTC)



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/application/project/project_repository.py
======================================================================

"""ProjectRepository — persistence port for the Project subsystem.

`ProjectRepository` is a Protocol. It defines the persistence operations
the Project Manager relies on, without committing to filesystem, database,
or remote storage. Concrete adapters live in `infrastructure/` (e.g.
`FilesystemProjectRepository`) or in the application layer for test
doubles (e.g. `InMemoryProjectRepository`).

Design rules (see `project.md` §11-§12):

- The repository is *not* the lifecycle authority. It does not decide
  whether a project should be created, opened, or deleted. It performs
  the operation and reports success or failure.
- The repository does not validate domain invariants. `Project` is
  already a valid domain aggregate when it reaches the repository.
- The repository does not publish events. Events belong to the
  Project Manager, which coordinates lifecycle transitions.
- The repository translates infrastructure failures into the
  `ProjectRepositoryError` family. Callers never see `OSError`,
  `FileNotFoundError`, or JSON `DecodeError`.
- `list_metadata` returns `ProjectSummary`, not `Project`. This is
  the read model used by the Home Page (`project.md` §5, §7): it
  must not load clinical data.
- `exists` is a cheap, side-effect-free probe. The Project Manager
  uses it to detect races before `create`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol, runtime_checkable

from cranioz.domain.project.project import Project
from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId
from cranioz.domain.project.project_summary import ProjectSummary


@runtime_checkable
class ProjectRepository(Protocol):
    """Persistence port for the Project subsystem.

    Implementations must raise the errors from
    `cranioz.application.project.project_errors` for every documented
    failure mode. They must not raise `KeyError`, `ValueError`, or any
    `OSError` to the caller.
    """

    def create(self, project: Project) -> None:
        """Persist a new project.

        Raises:
            ProjectAlreadyExists: a project with the same `project_id`
                is already persisted. The repository does not overwrite.
            ProjectStorageError: the underlying storage rejected the
                operation (permissions, disk full, I/O error, ...).
        """
        ...

    def get(self, project_id: ProjectId) -> Project:
        """Load and return a project by id.

        Raises:
            ProjectNotFound: no project with the given id is persisted.
            ProjectCorrupted: the persisted representation cannot be
                loaded as a valid `Project`.
            ProjectStorageError: the underlying storage failed while
                reading (permissions, I/O error, ...).
        """
        ...

    def update(self, project: Project) -> None:
        """Replace the persisted representation of an existing project.

        The `project.project_id` is the key. The repository does not
        create a project through `update`; it requires the project to
        already exist.

        Raises:
            ProjectNotFound: no project with the given id is persisted.
            ProjectStorageError: the underlying storage rejected the
                operation.
        """
        ...

    def delete(self, project_id: ProjectId) -> None:
        """Remove a project and its persisted representation.

        The operation is idempotent only at the *documented* level: the
        repository may raise `ProjectNotFound` if the project does not
        exist. Callers that want idempotency check `exists` first. This
        distinction lets the Project Manager distinguish "deleted now"
        from "already gone" when coordinating lifecycle events.

        Raises:
            ProjectNotFound: no project with the given id is persisted.
            ProjectStorageError: the underlying storage rejected the
                operation.
        """
        ...

    def exists(self, project_id: ProjectId) -> bool:
        """Return True if a project with the given id is persisted.

        A cheap, side-effect-free probe. Never raises for a missing
        project; that is the point. May raise `ProjectStorageError`
        if the underlying storage cannot be queried at all (e.g. the
        projects root is inaccessible).
        """
        ...

    def list_metadata(self) -> tuple[ProjectSummary, ...]:
        """Return lightweight summaries of all persisted projects.

        Returns a read-model projection (`ProjectSummary`), not full
        `Project` aggregates. The Home Page (`project.md` §7) relies
        on this contract: listing projects must not load clinical data.

        The order is unspecified; callers that need a particular order
        sort the tuple themselves. An empty tuple is returned when no
        project is persisted, not an error.

        Raises:
            ProjectStorageError: the underlying storage cannot be
                listed (e.g. the projects root is inaccessible).
        """
        ...

    def list_ids(self) -> tuple[ProjectId, ...]:
        """Return the ids of all persisted projects.

        A cheaper alternative to `list_metadata` for callers that only
        need identity (e.g. a completeness check in the Project
        Manager). Order is unspecified.

        Raises:
            ProjectStorageError: the underlying storage cannot be
                listed.
        """
        ...

======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/config/loader.py
======================================================================

"""ConfigurationLoader.

Loads system configuration from src/cranioz/config/defaults/.
Does not load user preferences; that is the responsibility of settings/.

Rules:
- The loader reads the defaults packaged with the application.
- The loader does not resolve physical OS paths.
- The loader returns a validated Config.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

from cranioz.config.schema import Config, ConfigError, build_config

_DEFAULTS_DIR = Path(__file__).parent / "defaults"


class ConfigurationLoader:
    """Loads and validates the system configuration."""

    def __init__(self, defaults_dir: Path | None = None) -> None:
        self._defaults_dir = defaults_dir or _DEFAULTS_DIR

    def load(self) -> Config:
        """Load app.json and paths.json, validate, and return a Config."""
        app_data = self._read_json("app.json")
        paths_data = self._read_json("paths.json")

        try:
            return build_config(app_data, paths_data)
        except ConfigError:
            raise
        except Exception as exc:
            raise ConfigError(f"failed to build configuration: {exc}") from exc

    def _read_json(self, filename: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
        path = self._defaults_dir / filename
        if not path.exists():
            raise ConfigError(f"configuration file not found: {path}")

        try:
            with path.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
                data = json.load(fh)
        except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
            raise ConfigError(f"invalid JSON in {path}: {exc}") from exc

        if not isinstance(data, dict):
            raise ConfigError(f"unexpected content in {path}: expected object")
        return data



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/config/migration.py
======================================================================

"""Configuration schema migration.

In this phase, the schema is 1 and there are no migrations.
This class exists as a future extension point.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any

CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1


class ConfigMigrationError(Exception):
    """Error during configuration migration."""


class ConfigMigrator:
    def migrate(self, data: dict[str, Any], from_version: int) -> dict[str, Any]:
        if from_version == CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION:
            return data

        if from_version > CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION:
            raise ConfigMigrationError(
                f"schema {from_version} is newer than supported ({CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION})"
            )

        # Extension point for future migrations.
        raise ConfigMigrationError(
            f"no migration defined from schema {from_version} to {CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION}"
        )



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/config/schema.py
======================================================================

"""Configuration schema and validation.

Defines required keys and accepted types.
No external dependencies; explicit validation only.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any


class ConfigError(Exception):
    """Configuration error: missing key, invalid type, or inconsistent value."""


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class AppConfig:
    name: str
    language: str
    theme: str
    recent_projects_limit: int


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PathsConfig:
    project_root: str | None
    flows_root: str | None
    cache_root: str | None


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class LoggingConfig:
    level: str
    format: str


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Config:
    app: AppConfig
    paths: PathsConfig
    logging: LoggingConfig


def _require(data: dict[str, Any], key: str, context: str) -> Any:
    if key not in data:
        raise ConfigError(f"missing key: {context}.{key}")
    return data[key]


def _require_type(value: Any, expected: type | tuple[type, ...], context: str) -> None:
    if not isinstance(value, expected):
        raise ConfigError(f"invalid type at {context}: expected {expected}, got {type(value)}")


def build_config(app_data: dict[str, Any], paths_data: dict[str, Any]) -> Config:
    app_section = _require(app_data, "app", "root")
    _require_type(app_section, dict, "root.app")

    logging_section = _require(app_data, "logging", "root")
    _require_type(logging_section, dict, "root.logging")

    paths_section = _require(paths_data, "paths", "root")
    _require_type(paths_section, dict, "root.paths")

    app = AppConfig(
        name=_require(app_section, "name", "app"),
        language=_require(app_section, "language", "app"),
        theme=_require(app_section, "theme", "app"),
        recent_projects_limit=_require(app_section, "recent_projects_limit", "app"),
    )

    _require_type(app.recent_projects_limit, int, "app.recent_projects_limit")

    logging_cfg = LoggingConfig(
        level=_require(logging_section, "level", "logging"),
        format=_require(logging_section, "format", "logging"),
    )

    paths = PathsConfig(
        project_root=paths_section.get("project_root"),
        flows_root=paths_section.get("flows_root"),
        cache_root=paths_section.get("cache_root"),
    )

    return Config(app=app, paths=paths, logging=logging_cfg)
