FILE: src/cranioz/infrastructure/app/__init__.py
======================================================================




======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/infrastructure/i18n/catalog_loader.py
======================================================================

"""CatalogLoader.

Loads translation catalog files from disk.

A catalog is a JSON file organized by language and functional area,
for example:

    translations/en/core.json
    translations/pt-BR/workspace.json

This module is intentionally minimal:

- It reads and parses a single JSON file.
- It does not cache.
- It does not decide which catalogs to load.
- It does not know about the TranslationService or the registry.

Caching and decision-making belong to the TranslationCatalogRegistry.

The loader does not validate the catalog structure beyond what the
JSON parser requires. Semantic validation is the responsibility of
the TranslationValidator (a separate tool, not part of the runtime).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any


class CatalogLoaderError(Exception):
    """Raised when a catalog file cannot be read or parsed."""


class CatalogLoader:
    """Loads translation catalog files from a base directory.

    The base directory is the root of the translations tree, for
    example: src/cranioz/translations.
    """

    def __init__(self, base_dir: Path) -> None:
        if not base_dir.exists():
            raise CatalogLoaderError(f"catalog base directory not found: {base_dir}")
        if not base_dir.is_dir():
            raise CatalogLoaderError(f"catalog base path is not a directory: {base_dir}")
        self._base_dir = base_dir

    @property
    def base_dir(self) -> Path:
        """Return the base directory for translation catalogs."""
        return self._base_dir

    def load(self, language: str, area: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Load a catalog for the given language and functional area.

        Args:
            language: BCP 47 language identifier (e.g. "en", "pt-BR").
            area: functional area (e.g. "core", "workspace").

        Returns:
            The parsed JSON contents as a dict.

        Raises:
            CatalogLoaderError: if the file is missing, unreadable,
                malformed, or does not contain a JSON object.
        """
        if not language:
            raise CatalogLoaderError("language must not be empty")
        if not area:
            raise CatalogLoaderError("area must not be empty")

        path = self._base_dir / language / f"{area}.json"

        if not path.exists():
            raise CatalogLoaderError(f"catalog file not found: {path}")

        try:
            with path.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
                data = json.load(fh)
        except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
            raise CatalogLoaderError(f"invalid JSON in {path}: {exc}") from exc
        except OSError as exc:
            raise CatalogLoaderError(f"cannot read {path}: {exc}") from exc

        if not isinstance(data, dict):
            raise CatalogLoaderError(f"catalog must be a JSON object: {path}")
        return data



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/infrastructure/i18n/catalog_registry.py
======================================================================

"""TranslationCatalogRegistry.

Locates, loads, and caches translation catalogs by language and
functional area.

Design rules:

- Catalogs are loaded on demand. Nothing is loaded at construction.
- Loaded catalogs are cached in memory, keyed by (language, area).
- A failed load is not cached: the next request will retry.
- The registry does not decide the active language. That is the
  TranslationService's responsibility.
- The registry does not merge catalogs. It returns the catalog for
  a single (language, area) pair. The TranslationService decides how
  to resolve a fully qualified key across areas.
- The registry supports explicit cache invalidation for development
  and testing.

The registry is a thin layer over the CatalogLoader. Its only added
value is caching and the (language, area) key.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any

from cranioz.infrastructure.i18n.catalog_loader import (
    CatalogLoader,
    CatalogLoaderError,
)


class CatalogRegistryError(Exception):
    """Raised when a catalog cannot be resolved."""


class TranslationCatalogRegistry:
    """Caches translation catalogs by (language, area)."""

    def __init__(self, loader: CatalogLoader) -> None:
        self._loader = loader
        self._cache: dict[tuple[str, str], dict[str, Any]] = {}

    # ----- Public API -----------------------------------------------

    def get(self, language: str, area: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Return the catalog for (language, area).

        Loads the catalog on first request and caches it. Subsequent
        requests for the same pair return the cached instance.

        Raises:
            CatalogRegistryError: if the catalog cannot be loaded.
        """
        key = (language, area)
        cached = self._cache.get(key)
        if cached is not None:
            return cached

        try:
            catalog = self._loader.load(language, area)
        except CatalogLoaderError as exc:
            raise CatalogRegistryError(
                f"cannot load catalog for language={language!r} area={area!r}: {exc}"
            ) from exc

        self._cache[key] = catalog
        return catalog

    def has(self, language: str, area: str) -> bool:
        """Return True if the catalog is currently cached."""
        return (language, area) in self._cache

    def invalidate(self, language: str, area: str) -> None:
        """Remove a single entry from the cache.

        No-op if the entry is not cached.
        """
        self._cache.pop((language, area), None)

    def clear(self) -> None:
        """Remove all cached catalogs.

        Used during development and testing, or when the underlying
        catalog files change on disk and need to be reloaded.
        """
        self._cache.clear()

    def cached_keys(self) -> tuple[tuple[str, str], ...]:
        """Return the (language, area) pairs currently cached."""
        return tuple(self._cache.keys())



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/infrastructure/i18n/instance.py
======================================================================

"""Global TranslationService instance (controlled singleton).

Widgets and UI code call ``get_translation_service()`` directly,
without receiving the service through a constructor. This avoids
threading the service through every widget's __init__.

To keep tests deterministic, the singleton is *controlled*:

- ``configure(service)`` replaces the current instance.
- ``reset()`` restores the default (an IdentityTranslationService).
- A test can install a controlled service in a fixture and reset
  it at teardown.

The module does not do any I/O at import time. The default instance
is created lazily on first access.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from cranioz.infrastructure.i18n.translation_service import (
    IdentityTranslationService,
    TranslationService,
)

_instance: TranslationService | None = None


def get_translation_service() -> TranslationService:
    """Return the current global TranslationService.

    If no service has been configured yet, a default
    IdentityTranslationService is created and stored. The default
    returns the source text unchanged, which is safe: it never
    returns an internal key.
    """
    global _instance  # noqa: PLW0603
    if _instance is None:
        _instance = IdentityTranslationService()
    return _instance


def configure(service: TranslationService) -> None:
    """Install a new global TranslationService.

    Typically called once at application boot, after the catalogs
    have been discovered. It may also be called from tests to install
    a service backed by a controlled registry.

    Replaces the previous instance. The previous instance is not
    disposed: the TranslationService contract has no dispose method.
    """
    global _instance  # noqa: PLW0603
    _instance = service


def reset() -> None:
    """Restore the default (IdentityTranslationService).

    Intended for tests: a fixture may call reset() at teardown so
    that subsequent tests do not see a leaked service.
    """
    global _instance  # noqa: PLW0603
    _instance = None



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/infrastructure/i18n/translation_service.py
======================================================================

"""TranslationService.

Resolves translation keys to localized strings.

This module defines:

- The ``TranslationService`` contract, as a Protocol.
- A concrete implementation, ``CatalogTranslationService``, backed
  by a TranslationCatalogRegistry.
- An ``IdentityTranslationService``, useful for tests and for the
  early phase of the application before catalogs exist.

The public API is a single method::

    service.tr(key, source_text, **params)

The ``source_text`` argument is mandatory (see the i18n documentation
§5). It provides a last-resort fallback when the key cannot be
resolved through the catalogs.

Fallback sequence (i18n doc §8):

1. Look up the message in the active language catalog.
2. If unavailable, look up the message in the English source catalog.
3. If unavailable, use the required English ``source_text``.
4. If no usable text can be produced, use a safe generic message.

Today the service supports simple named-parameter interpolation
("Hello {name}"). ICU MessageFormat (pluralization, select, etc.) is
intentionally not implemented yet: no message in the codebase
requires it. It will be added when the first plural message appears.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from typing import Any, Protocol, runtime_checkable

from cranioz.infrastructure.i18n.catalog_registry import (
    CatalogRegistryError,
    TranslationCatalogRegistry,
)

_logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

SOURCE_LANGUAGE = "en"

# A safe generic message used as a last-resort fallback (step 4 of
# the fallback sequence). It is intentionally not translated: if the
# translation system cannot produce text, we must not depend on it.
_GENERIC_FALLBACK = "[translation unavailable]"


@runtime_checkable
class TranslationService(Protocol):
    """Contract for a translation service.

    Implementations must be able to:
    - return the active language;
    - change the active language;
    - resolve a translation key with a mandatory English source text.
    """

    @property
    def language(self) -> str: ...

    def set_language(self, language: str) -> None: ...

    def tr(self, key: str, source_text: str, **params: Any) -> str: ...


class CatalogTranslationService:
    """TranslationService backed by a TranslationCatalogRegistry.

    Keys are namespaced by functional area:

        "core.app.name"       → area "core",     path ["app", "name"]
        "workspace.area.x"    → area "workspace", path ["area", "x"]

    The first segment of the key selects the catalog. The remaining
    segments navigate the catalog tree to find an entry with a
    "message" field.

    If the key cannot be resolved through the active language catalog,
    the English catalog is tried. If that fails too, the required
    ``source_text`` is used. If even that is empty, a generic fallback
    message is returned and a diagnostic is logged.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        registry: TranslationCatalogRegistry,
        language: str = SOURCE_LANGUAGE,
    ) -> None:
        if not language:
            raise ValueError("language must not be empty")
        self._registry = registry
        self._language = language

    # ----- Language management --------------------------------------

    @property
    def language(self) -> str:
        return self._language

    def set_language(self, language: str) -> None:
        """Change the active language.

        No catalogs are loaded here: they are loaded on demand by the
        registry. Changing the language is therefore cheap.

        Raises ValueError if the language is empty.
        """
        if not language:
            raise ValueError("language must not be empty")
        self._language = language

    # ----- Translation ----------------------------------------------

    def tr(self, key: str, source_text: str, **params: Any) -> str:
        """Resolve a translation key.

        Args:
            key: semantic translation key, for example
                "workspace.area.placeholder.empty".
            source_text: mandatory English source text. Used as the
                last-resort fallback when the key is not resolvable
                through the catalogs.
            **params: named parameters for interpolation.

        Returns:
            The localized string, with parameters interpolated.
        """
        if not key:
            raise ValueError("key must not be empty")

        message = self._resolve(key, source_text)

        try:
            return message.format(**params)
        except (KeyError, IndexError, ValueError) as exc:
            _logger.warning("translation interpolation failed for key=%r: %s", key, exc)
            # Interpolation failure is a fallback event. Return the
            # source text unformatted so the user still sees something.
            return source_text or _GENERIC_FALLBACK

    # ----- Internals ------------------------------------------------

    def _resolve(self, key: str, source_text: str) -> str:
        """Apply the fallback sequence to resolve a key to a message."""
        message = self._lookup(self._language, key)
        if message is not None:
            return message

        if self._language != SOURCE_LANGUAGE:
            message = self._lookup(SOURCE_LANGUAGE, key)
            if message is not None:
                return message

        if source_text:
            return source_text

        _logger.error(
            "no usable translation for key=%r (language=%r)",
            key,
            self._language,
        )
        return _GENERIC_FALLBACK

    def _lookup(self, language: str, key: str) -> str | None:
        """Look up a key in the catalog of the given language.

        Returns the message string, or None if the catalog or key is
        unavailable.
        """
        area, *path = key.split(".")
        if not area or not path:
            return None

        try:
            catalog = self._registry.get(language, area)
        except CatalogRegistryError:
            return None

        node: Any = catalog
        for segment in path:
            if not isinstance(node, dict):
                return None
            node = node.get(segment)
            if node is None:
                return None

        if isinstance(node, dict):
            message = node.get("message")
            if isinstance(message, str):
                return message

        return None


class IdentityTranslationService:
    """TranslationService that returns the source_text unchanged.

    Useful for:
    - Tests that do not care about translations.
    - The early phase of the application before catalogs exist.
    - Fallback when no catalog registry is available.

    It supports the language property and set_language() so that
    callers see a consistent API, but changing the language has no
    effect on the returned text.
    """

    def __init__(self, language: str = SOURCE_LANGUAGE) -> None:
        if not language:
            raise ValueError("language must not be empty")
        self._language = language

    @property
    def language(self) -> str:
        return self._language

    def set_language(self, language: str) -> None:
        if not language:
            raise ValueError("language must not be empty")
        self._language = language

    def tr(self, key: str, source_text: str, **params: Any) -> str:
        if not key:
            raise ValueError("key must not be empty")
        try:
            return source_text.format(**params)
        except (KeyError, IndexError, ValueError):
            return source_text or _GENERIC_FALLBACK



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/infrastructure/i18n/__init__.py
======================================================================

"""CranioZ internationalization (i18n) infrastructure.

Public entry points:

- ``get_translation_service()``: return the active global service.
- ``configure(service)``: install a service (typically at boot).
- ``reset()``: restore the default (used by tests).
- ``tr(key, source_text, **params)``: convenience wrapper that calls
  the active service.

Usage in UI code::

    from cranioz.infrastructure.i18n import tr

    label = QLabel(tr("core.app.name", "CranioZ"))
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any

from cranioz.infrastructure.i18n.instance import (
    configure,
    get_translation_service,
    reset,
)
from cranioz.infrastructure.i18n.translation_service import (
    CatalogTranslationService,
    IdentityTranslationService,
    TranslationService,
)

__all__ = [
    "CatalogTranslationService",
    "IdentityTranslationService",
    "TranslationService",
    "configure",
    "get_translation_service",
    "reset",
    "tr",
]


def tr(key: str, source_text: str, **params: Any) -> str:
    """Convenience wrapper around the active TranslationService.

    Calls ``get_translation_service().tr(key, source_text, **params)``.
    """
    return get_translation_service().tr(key, source_text, **params)



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/infrastructure/project/filesystem_project_repository.py
======================================================================

"""FilesystemProjectRepository — filesystem adapter for ProjectRepository.

Implements the `ProjectRepository` port (Peça 7.1) over a directory tree:

    <projects_root>/
    └── <project_id>/
        ├── project.json        (owned by this repository)
        └── patient_record.json (owned by the Patient persistence layer;
                                 not read or written here)

The repository uses `ProjectPathResolver` for path resolution and never
constructs filesystem paths manually. `ProjectPathResolver` guarantees
that every resolved path stays inside the project directory, so this
repository cannot be tricked into writing outside the projects root.

Design rules (see `project.md` §11-§12):

- No lifecycle decisions. `create` creates; `delete` deletes. The
  Project Manager (Peça 7.5) coordinates *when* to call them.
- Every infrastructure failure is translated into the
  `ProjectRepositoryError` family. Callers never see `OSError`,
  `FileNotFoundError`, or JSON `DecodeError`.
- `delete` raises `ProjectNotFound` when the project is missing. This
  matches the contract defined in Peça 7.1.
- `exists` checks `project.json`, not the directory. The directory may
  exist because of files owned by other layers; from the repository's
  point of view, a project exists iff its `project.json` is present.
- `delete` removes only `project.json`. Other files belong to other
  persistence layers, which own their own lifecycle.
- `create` is atomic at the level of the visible project directory:
  it writes `project.json` first, then renames into place. A crash
  mid-write leaves no partially-created project that `list_metadata`
  would see.
- `list_metadata` skips corrupted entries and logs them; one bad
  project does not break the Home Page.

Threading: not thread-safe. The Project Manager runs on the main
thread and is the only caller.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import logging
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

from cranioz.application.project.project_errors import (
    ProjectAlreadyExists,
    ProjectCorrupted,
    ProjectNotFound,
    ProjectStorageError,
)
from cranioz.domain.project.project import Project
from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId
from cranioz.domain.project.project_summary import ProjectSummary
from cranioz.infrastructure.project.project_path_resolver import (
    PROJECT_FILE_NAME,
    ProjectPathInaccessible,
    ProjectPathResolver,
)
from cranioz.infrastructure.project.project_serialization import (
    ProjectSerializationError,
    project_from_dict,
    project_to_dict,
)

_logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class FilesystemProjectRepository:
    """Filesystem-backed implementation of `ProjectRepository`."""

    def __init__(self, projects_root: Path) -> None:
        self._projects_root = Path(projects_root)

    # ----- create ----------------------------------------------------

    def create(self, project: Project) -> None:
        resolver = self._resolver_for(project.project_id)
        project_dir = resolver.project_dir

        # exists() is the authoritative check: a directory without
        # project.json is not a project (it may be a stray directory).
        if resolver.project_file.is_file():
            raise ProjectAlreadyExists(
                f"project already exists: {project.project_id.value!r}",
                project_id=project.project_id,
            )

        try:
            project_dir.mkdir(parents=False, exist_ok=True)
        except OSError as exc:
            raise ProjectStorageError(
                f"cannot create project directory: {project_dir}: {exc}",
                project_id=project.project_id,
            ) from exc

        try:
            self._write_project_json(resolver, project)
        except Exception:
            # Rollback: if we created the directory, remove it. If the
            # directory existed (owned by another layer), leave it alone.
            try:
                if project_dir.is_dir():
                    project_json = resolver.project_file
                    if not project_json.exists():
                        project_dir.rmdir()
            except OSError:
                _logger.exception(
                    "could not roll back project directory after failed create: %s",
                    project_dir,
                )
            raise

    # ----- get -------------------------------------------------------

    def get(self, project_id: ProjectId) -> Project:
        resolver = self._resolver_for(project_id)

        if not resolver.project_file.is_file():
            raise ProjectNotFound(
                f"project not found: {project_id.value!r}",
                project_id=project_id,
            )

        data = self._read_project_json(resolver, project_id)

        try:
            return project_from_dict(data)
        except ProjectSerializationError as exc:
            raise ProjectCorrupted(
                f"project is corrupted: {project_id.value!r}: {exc}",
                project_id=project_id,
            ) from exc

    # ----- update ----------------------------------------------------

    def update(self, project: Project) -> None:
        resolver = self._resolver_for(project.project_id)

        if not resolver.project_file.is_file():
            raise ProjectNotFound(
                f"project not found: {project.project_id.value!r}",
                project_id=project.project_id,
            )

        self._write_project_json(resolver, project)

    # ----- delete ----------------------------------------------------

    def delete(self, project_id: ProjectId) -> None:
        resolver = self._resolver_for(project_id)

        if not resolver.project_file.is_file():
            raise ProjectNotFound(
                f"project not found: {project_id.value!r}",
                project_id=project_id,
            )

        try:
            resolver.project_file.unlink()
        except OSError as exc:
            raise ProjectStorageError(
                f"cannot delete project file: {exc}",
                project_id=project_id,
            ) from exc

    # ----- exists ----------------------------------------------------

    def exists(self, project_id: ProjectId) -> bool:
        resolver = self._resolver_for(project_id)
        return resolver.project_file.is_file()

    # ----- list_metadata --------------------------------------------

    def list_metadata(self) -> tuple[ProjectSummary, ...]:
        summaries: list[ProjectSummary] = []
        for project_id in self.list_ids():
            try:
                project = self.get(project_id)
            except ProjectCorrupted:
                _logger.exception(
                    "skipping corrupted project while listing: %s",
                    project_id.value,
                )
                continue
            except ProjectNotFound:
                # Race: project disappeared between list_ids and get.
                continue
            except ProjectStorageError:
                _logger.exception(
                    "cannot read project while listing: %s",
                    project_id.value,
                )
                continue
            summaries.append(ProjectSummary.from_metadata(project.metadata))
        return tuple(summaries)

    # ----- list_ids --------------------------------------------------

    def list_ids(self) -> tuple[ProjectId, ...]:
        if not self._projects_root.is_dir():
            return ()

        try:
            entries = list(self._projects_root.iterdir())
        except OSError as exc:
            raise ProjectStorageError(
                f"cannot list projects root: {self._projects_root}: {exc}"
            ) from exc

        ids: list[ProjectId] = []
        for entry in entries:
            if not entry.is_dir():
                continue
            if not (entry / PROJECT_FILE_NAME).is_file():
                # Directory exists but no project.json: not a project.
                continue
            try:
                ids.append(ProjectId(entry.name))
            except ValueError:
                # Not a valid ProjectId; skip.
                continue
        return tuple(ids)

    # ----- Internals -------------------------------------------------

    def _resolver_for(self, project_id: ProjectId) -> ProjectPathResolver:
        try:
            return ProjectPathResolver(
                projects_root=self._projects_root,
                project_id=project_id,
            )
        except ProjectPathInaccessible as exc:
            raise ProjectStorageError(
                f"invalid project_id for filesystem: {project_id.value!r}: {exc}",
                project_id=project_id,
            ) from exc

    def _read_project_json(
        self,
        resolver: ProjectPathResolver,
        project_id: ProjectId,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        project_json = resolver.project_file
        try:
            with project_json.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
                data = json.load(fh)
        except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
            raise ProjectCorrupted(
                f"invalid JSON in {project_json}: {exc}",
                project_id=project_id,
            ) from exc
        except OSError as exc:
            raise ProjectStorageError(
                f"cannot read {project_json}: {exc}",
                project_id=project_id,
            ) from exc

        if not isinstance(data, dict):
            raise ProjectCorrupted(
                f"project file must be a JSON object: {project_json}",
                project_id=project_id,
            )
        return data

    def _write_project_json(
        self,
        resolver: ProjectPathResolver,
        project: Project,
    ) -> None:
        data = project_to_dict(project)
        project_json = resolver.project_file

        # Write to a temporary file first, then rename, so that a
        # crash mid-write does not leave a partial project.json.
        tmp = project_json.with_suffix(project_json.suffix + ".tmp")
        try:
            with tmp.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
                json.dump(data, fh, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)
                fh.write("\n")
            tmp.replace(project_json)
        except OSError as exc:
            try:
                if tmp.exists():
                    tmp.unlink()
            except OSError:
                _logger.exception("could not remove temporary file: %s", tmp)
            raise ProjectStorageError(
                f"cannot write {project_json}: {exc}",
                project_id=project.project_id,
            ) from exc



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/infrastructure/project/project_path_resolver.py
======================================================================

"""ProjectPathResolver.

Resolves file paths inside a specific CranioZ project.

The resolver is initialized with two pieces of information:

- The projects root, provided by ``AppPathResolver.projects_root``.
- The ``ProjectId`` of the project being opened.

From those, it derives every path that belongs to the project:

    <projects_root>/<project_id>/
    <projects_root>/<project_id>/project.json
    <projects_root>/<project_id>/patient_record.json
    <projects_root>/<project_id>/workflow.json
    <projects_root>/<project_id>/imaging/ct.dcm
    <projects_root>/<project_id>/models/maxilla.stl
    ...

The resolver does NOT touch the filesystem beyond checking whether
the project directory exists (``exists()``). It does not create
directories, does not read files, and does not write anything. Those
responsibilities belong to the ProjectRepository.

Rules:

- The ``ProjectId`` value must be a safe path component. Values
  containing ``/``, ``\\``, ``..``, ``:``, control characters, or
  starting with ``.`` are rejected at construction time.
- Every path returned by this resolver is guaranteed to be inside
  the project directory. Traversal attempts through ``resource()``
  raise ``ProjectPathInaccessible``.
- The resolver is stateless after construction. It can be reused
  for the lifetime of the project.

See also:

- ``infrastructure/app/app_path_resolver.py`` — application-level
  paths (projects root, flows root, cache root).
- ADR-0004 and ``project_pt.md`` §14 — the design rationale for
  separating application paths from project paths.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path

from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId

# Standard file names inside a project directory. Kept as module
# constants so they can be referenced in tests and in the
# repository without risk of typo.
PROJECT_FILE_NAME = "project.json"
PATIENT_RECORD_FILE_NAME = "patient_record.json"
WORKFLOW_FILE_NAME = "workflow.json"


class ProjectPathInaccessible(Exception):
    """Raised when a project path cannot be resolved."""


class ProjectPathResolver:
    """Resolves file paths inside a specific CranioZ project.

    The resolver is built once per project, typically when the
    ProjectManager opens the project. It does not touch the disk
    except when ``exists()`` is called.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        projects_root: Path,
        project_id: ProjectId,
    ) -> None:
        self._validate_project_id(project_id)
        self._projects_root = projects_root
        self._project_id = project_id

    # ----- Identity ------------------------------------------------

    @property
    def projects_root(self) -> Path:
        """The root directory that contains all projects."""
        return self._projects_root

    @property
    def project_id(self) -> ProjectId:
        """The ProjectId this resolver was built for."""
        return self._project_id

    # ----- Standard paths ------------------------------------------

    @property
    def project_dir(self) -> Path:
        """Directory of this project: ``<projects_root>/<project_id>``."""
        return self._projects_root / self._project_id.value

    @property
    def project_file(self) -> Path:
        """The project.json file of this project."""
        return self.project_dir / PROJECT_FILE_NAME

    @property
    def patient_record_file(self) -> Path:
        """The patient_record.json file of this project."""
        return self.project_dir / PATIENT_RECORD_FILE_NAME

    @property
    def workflow_file(self) -> Path:
        """The workflow.json file of this project."""
        return self.project_dir / WORKFLOW_FILE_NAME

    # ----- Free-form resources -------------------------------------

    def resource(self, relative: str) -> Path:
        """Resolve a logical relative path inside the project.

        Accepts POSIX-style (``"imaging/ct.dcm"``) and Windows-style
        (``"imaging\\ct.dcm"``) separators. Both are normalized to
        the platform's separator internally.

        The returned path is guaranteed to be inside the project
        directory. Traversal attempts (``..``) or absolute paths
        raise ``ProjectPathInaccessible``.

        The method does not check whether the target exists.
        """
        if not relative:
            raise ProjectPathInaccessible("relative path must not be empty")

        # Reject absolute paths in a platform-independent way.
        # Path(...).is_absolute() only detects platform-native
        # absolutes: on Windows it does not detect "/etc/passwd",
        # because a leading "/" is interpreted as the root of the
        # current drive. Since the resolver must behave the same on
        # every platform, we reject any leading separator and any
        # drive letter explicitly.
        if relative.startswith(("/", "\\")):
            raise ProjectPathInaccessible(f"relative path must not be absolute: {relative!r}")
        if len(relative) >= 2 and relative[1] == ":":
            # Windows drive letter, e.g. "C:...".
            raise ProjectPathInaccessible(f"relative path must not be absolute: {relative!r}")
        if Path(relative).is_absolute():
            # Covers any remaining platform-specific absolute form.
            raise ProjectPathInaccessible(f"relative path must not be absolute: {relative!r}")

        project_dir = self.project_dir.resolve()
        candidate = (project_dir / relative).resolve()

        if project_dir not in candidate.parents and candidate != project_dir:
            raise ProjectPathInaccessible(f"path escapes project directory: {relative!r}")
        return candidate

    # ----- State ---------------------------------------------------

    def exists(self) -> bool:
        """Return True if the project directory exists on disk.

        Does not check for individual files: the caller may want to
        inspect the project directory even if some files are missing.
        """
        return self.project_dir.is_dir()

    # ----- Validation ----------------------------------------------

    @staticmethod
    def _validate_project_id(project_id: ProjectId) -> None:
        """Reject ProjectIds that cannot be used as a path component.

        The ProjectId is a domain-level concept; it may contain any
        string the domain allows. This resolver, however, is about
        to embed it into a filesystem path, so it must be a safe
        single path segment.

        Rejected patterns:

        - empty or whitespace-only
        - contains ``/`` or ``\\`` (path separators)
        - contains ``..`` (traversal)
        - contains ``:`` (Windows drive separator, NTFS stream
          separator)
        - contains ASCII control characters (0x00-0x1F)
        - starts with ``.`` (would produce hidden directories, and
          would allow ``.`` or ``..`` themselves)
        """
        value = project_id.value

        if not value or not value.strip():
            raise ProjectPathInaccessible("ProjectId value must not be empty or whitespace only")
        if "/" in value or "\\" in value:
            raise ProjectPathInaccessible(
                f"ProjectId value must not contain path separators: {value!r}"
            )
        if ".." in value:
            raise ProjectPathInaccessible(f"ProjectId value must not contain '..': {value!r}")
        if ":" in value:
            raise ProjectPathInaccessible(f"ProjectId value must not contain ':': {value!r}")
        if any(ord(ch) < 0x20 for ch in value):
            raise ProjectPathInaccessible(
                f"ProjectId value must not contain control characters: {value!r}"
            )
        if value.startswith("."):
            raise ProjectPathInaccessible(f"ProjectId value must not start with '.': {value!r}")



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/infrastructure/project/project_serialization.py
======================================================================

"""Serialization of Project to/from JSON-compatible dictionaries.

Pure functions. No I/O, no filesystem, no repository. The functions
here define the *shape* of the persisted representation; the repository
(Peça 7.3) handles file reading/writing and error translation.

Design rules (see `project.md` §13):

- The serialized form is versioned (`schema_version`). Migrations are
  the responsibility of a dedicated migrator; this module only knows
  the current version.
- Timestamps are ISO 8601 with timezone (`+00:00`), matching the
  domain's `datetime` with `tzinfo=UTC`.
- Enums are serialized by their `.value` string, not by `.name`. The
  values are stable identifiers (`"active"`, not `"ACTIVE"`).
- The metadata's project_id is the canonical key; it must match the
  directory name (`ProjectPathResolver.project_id`).
- `patient_record.json` is NOT serialized here. The Repository does
  not own the Patient record; it only stores the reference
  (`patient_id`) and the denormalized `patient_display_name` used by
  the read model.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import datetime
from typing import Any

from cranioz.domain.project.project import Project
from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId
from cranioz.domain.project.project_metadata import ProjectMetadata
from cranioz.domain.project.project_status import ProjectStatus

# The current version of the persisted representation. Bumped whenever
# the shape of project.json changes in a way that requires migration.
CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1


class ProjectSerializationError(ValueError):
    """Raised when a dict cannot be interpreted as a valid Project."""


# ----- Encode --------------------------------------------------------


def project_to_dict(project: Project) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Return a JSON-compatible dict for the given Project."""
    md = project.metadata
    return {
        "schema_version": md.schema_version,
        "project_id": project.project_id.value,
        "project_name": md.project_name,
        "patient_id": md.patient_id,
        "patient_display_name": md.patient_display_name,
        "clinical_procedure": md.clinical_procedure,
        "status": md.status.value,
        "created_at": _datetime_to_iso(md.created_at),
        "updated_at": _datetime_to_iso(md.updated_at),
        "last_opened_at": (
            _datetime_to_iso(md.last_opened_at) if md.last_opened_at is not None else None
        ),
    }


# ----- Decode --------------------------------------------------------


def project_from_dict(data: dict[str, Any]) -> Project:
    """Reconstruct a Project from a dict.

    Raises:
        ProjectSerializationError: the dict is missing required keys,
            has invalid types, or fails a domain invariant.
    """
    if not isinstance(data, dict):
        raise ProjectSerializationError(
            f"project data must be a dict, got {type(data).__name__}"
        )

    schema_version = _require_int(data, "schema_version")
    if schema_version != CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION:
        raise ProjectSerializationError(
            f"unsupported schema_version: {schema_version} "
            f"(expected {CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION})"
        )

    try:
        project_id = ProjectId(_require_str(data, "project_id"))
        metadata = ProjectMetadata(
            project_id=project_id,
            project_name=_require_str(data, "project_name"),
            patient_id=_require_str(data, "patient_id"),
            patient_display_name=_require_str(data, "patient_display_name"),
            clinical_procedure=_require_str(data, "clinical_procedure"),
            status=_parse_status(_require_str(data, "status")),
            created_at=_parse_datetime(_require_str(data, "created_at"), "created_at"),
            updated_at=_parse_datetime(_require_str(data, "updated_at"), "updated_at"),
            last_opened_at=_parse_optional_datetime(data, "last_opened_at"),
            schema_version=schema_version,
        )
    except ProjectSerializationError:
        raise
    except Exception as exc:
        raise ProjectSerializationError(f"invalid project data: {exc}") from exc

    try:
        return Project(project_id=project_id, metadata=metadata)
    except ValueError as exc:
        raise ProjectSerializationError(f"invalid project: {exc}") from exc


# ----- Internals ----------------------------------------------------


def _require_str(data: dict[str, Any], key: str) -> str:
    if key not in data:
        raise ProjectSerializationError(f"missing required key: {key!r}")
    value = data[key]
    if not isinstance(value, str):
        raise ProjectSerializationError(
            f"invalid type for {key!r}: expected str, got {type(value).__name__}"
        )
    return value


def _require_int(data: dict[str, Any], key: str) -> int:
    if key not in data:
        raise ProjectSerializationError(f"missing required key: {key!r}")
    value = data[key]
    if not isinstance(value, int) or isinstance(value, bool):
        raise ProjectSerializationError(
            f"invalid type for {key!r}: expected int, got {type(value).__name__}"
        )
    return value


def _datetime_to_iso(value: datetime) -> str:
    return value.isoformat()


def _parse_datetime(value: str, field_name: str) -> datetime:
    try:
        parsed = datetime.fromisoformat(value)
    except ValueError as exc:
        raise ProjectSerializationError(
            f"invalid ISO 8601 for {field_name!r}: {value!r}"
        ) from exc
    if parsed.tzinfo is None:
        raise ProjectSerializationError(
            f"{field_name!r} must be timezone-aware, got {value!r}"
        )
    return parsed


def _parse_optional_datetime(data: dict[str, Any], key: str) -> datetime | None:
    value = data.get(key)
    if value is None:
        return None
    if not isinstance(value, str):
        raise ProjectSerializationError(
            f"invalid type for {key!r}: expected str or null, "
            f"got {type(value).__name__}"
        )
    return _parse_datetime(value, key)


def _parse_status(value: str) -> ProjectStatus:
    try:
        return ProjectStatus(value)
    except ValueError as exc:
        raise ProjectSerializationError(f"invalid project status: {value!r}") from exc

    PROJECT_FILE_NAME,
    WORKFLOW_FILE_NAME,
    ProjectPathInaccessible,
    ProjectPathResolver,
)

    CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION,
    ProjectSerializationError,
    project_from_dict,
    project_to_dict,
)

__all__ = [
    "CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION",
    "PATIENT_RECORD_FILE_NAME",
    "PROJECT_FILE_NAME",
    "WORKFLOW_FILE_NAME",

======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/main_window.py
======================================================================

"""MainWindow — the application shell.

MainWindow is the top-level application window. It hosts the Home
Page and the Workspace, and it is the sole orchestrator of navigation
between them (ADR-0006).

Responsibilities:

- Host the HomePage and the Workspace in a QStackedWidget.
- Listen to HomePage.project_open_requested; call ProjectManager,
  create the Workspace with the resulting ProjectContext, inject
  the "Home" button into the TopOverlay, and switch to the Workspace.
- Listen to the "Home" button's clicked; call ProjectManager.close_project,
  destroy the Workspace, and switch back to the HomePage.
- Forward other HomePage signals to MessageBox placeholders for
  pieces that are not yet implemented (Bloco 8 / Bloco 9 / 7.6d).

Non-responsibilities (see main_window.md §9 and ADR-0006):

- MainWindow does not implement project lifecycle rules.
- MainWindow does not implement workflow rules.
- MainWindow does not manage long-running operations.
- MainWindow does not persist state.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from PySide6.QtWidgets import (
    QMainWindow,
    QMessageBox,
    QPushButton,
    QStackedWidget,
    QWidget,
)

from cranioz.application.project.project_catalog import ProjectCatalog
from cranioz.application.project.project_context import ProjectContext
from cranioz.application.project.project_errors import ProjectError
from cranioz.application.project.project_manager import ProjectManager
from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId
from cranioz.ui.editors.editor_registry import EditorRegistry
from cranioz.ui.home.home_page import HomePage
from cranioz.ui.theme.service import ThemeService
from cranioz.ui.tools.registry.tool_registry import ToolRegistry
from cranioz.ui.workspace.workspace import Workspace
from cranioz.ui.workspace.workspace_spec import (
    DEFAULT as WORKSPACE_SPEC_DEFAULT,
)
from cranioz.ui.workspace.workspace_spec import (
    PLACEHOLDER_EDITOR_CLASS,
    PLACEHOLDER_EDITOR_ID,
)


class MainWindow(QMainWindow):
    """Application shell: hosts HomePage and Workspace."""

    def __init__(
            self,
            *,
            catalog: ProjectCatalog,
            manager: ProjectManager,
            actor: str,
            editor_registry: EditorRegistry,
            tool_registry: ToolRegistry,
            theme_service: ThemeService,
            parent: QWidget | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        super().__init__(parent)
        self._catalog = catalog
        self._manager = manager
        self._actor = actor
        self._editor_registry = editor_registry
        self._tool_registry = tool_registry
        self._theme_service = theme_service

        self._current_project_id: ProjectId | None = None
        self._workspace: Workspace | None = None

        # Register the placeholder editor used by WORKSPACE_SPEC_DEFAULT.
        # Idempotent: registering the same class twice is a no-op.
        self._editor_registry.register(PLACEHOLDER_EDITOR_ID, PLACEHOLDER_EDITOR_CLASS)

        self._home = HomePage(catalog=self._catalog, parent=self)
        self._stack = QStackedWidget(self)
        self._stack.addWidget(self._home)
        self.setCentralWidget(self._stack)

        self._connect_home_signals()

        self.setWindowTitle("CranioZ")
        self.resize(1280, 800)

    # ----- HomePage signal wiring ------------------------------------

    def _connect_home_signals(self) -> None:
        home = self._home

        home.project_open_requested.connect(self._on_project_open_requested)
        home.new_project_requested.connect(self._on_new_project_requested)
        home.manage_projects_requested.connect(self._on_manage_projects_requested)
        home.manage_flows_requested.connect(self._on_manage_flows_requested)
        home.settings_requested.connect(self._on_settings_requested)
        home.help_requested.connect(self._on_help_requested)

        home.community_requested.connect(self._on_community_requested)
        home.library_requested.connect(self._on_library_requested)
        home.news_requested.connect(self._on_news_requested)
        home.feedback_requested.connect(self._on_feedback_requested)
        home.contribute_requested.connect(self._on_contribute_requested)

    # ----- Project navigation ----------------------------------------

    def _on_project_open_requested(
        self,
        project_id: ProjectId,
        flow_id: str,
    ) -> None:
        try:
            context = self._manager.open_project(
                self._actor,
                project_id,
                workflow_id=flow_id,
                workflow_config={},
            )
        except ProjectError as exc:
            QMessageBox.critical(self, "Cannot open project", str(exc))
            return

        workspace = self._create_workspace(context)
        self._stack.addWidget(workspace)
        self._stack.setCurrentWidget(workspace)
        self._workspace = workspace
        self._current_project_id = project_id

    def _create_workspace(self, context: ProjectContext) -> Workspace:
        workspace = Workspace(
            editor_registry=self._editor_registry,
            tool_registry=self._tool_registry,
            theme_service=self._theme_service,
            project_context=context,
        )

        home_button = QPushButton("Home")
        home_button.setObjectName("workspace.home_button")
        home_button.clicked.connect(self._on_home_clicked)
        workspace.top_overlay().set_left_content(home_button)

        workspace.load_spec(WORKSPACE_SPEC_DEFAULT)
        return workspace

    def _on_home_clicked(self) -> None:
        if self._current_project_id is None or self._workspace is None:
            return

        self._manager.close_project(self._actor, self._current_project_id)
        self._stack.removeWidget(self._workspace)
        self._workspace.setParent(None)
        self._workspace.deleteLater()
        self._workspace = None
        self._current_project_id = None

        self._stack.setCurrentWidget(self._home)
        self._home.refresh()

    # ----- HomePage action handlers (placeholders) ------------------

    def _on_new_project_requested(self) -> None:
        QMessageBox.information(
            self,
            "New project",
            "Available after Bloco 8 (Patient Module).",
        )

    def _on_manage_projects_requested(self) -> None:
        QMessageBox.information(
            self,
            "Manage projects",
            "Available after Bloco 8 (Patient Module).",
        )

    def _on_manage_flows_requested(self) -> None:
        QMessageBox.information(
            self,
            "Manage flows",
            "Available after Bloco 9 (Flow Catalog).",
        )

    def _on_settings_requested(self) -> None:
        QMessageBox.information(
            self,
            "Settings",
            "Available after piece 7.6d (Settings).",
        )

    def _on_help_requested(self) -> None:
        QMessageBox.information(
            self,
            "Help",
            "Available after piece 7.6d (Documentation).",
        )

    def _on_community_requested(self) -> None:
        QMessageBox.information(
            self,
            "Community",
            "Available in a future piece.",
        )

    def _on_library_requested(self) -> None:
        QMessageBox.information(
            self,
            "Object library",
            "Available in a future piece.",
        )

    def _on_news_requested(self) -> None:
        QMessageBox.information(
            self,
            "News",
            "Available in a future piece.",
        )

    def _on_feedback_requested(self) -> None:
        QMessageBox.information(
            self,
            "Suggest improvements",
            "Available in a future piece.",
        )

    def _on_contribute_requested(self) -> None:
        QMessageBox.information(
            self,
            "Contribute",
            "Available in a future piece.",
        )

    # ----- Convenience for tests ------------------------------------

    def home(self) -> HomePage:
        return self._home

    def current_workspace(self) -> Workspace | None:
        return self._workspace

    def current_project_id(self) -> ProjectId | None:
        return self._current_project_id

======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/editors/base_editor.py

"""Editor ABC.

An Editor is a functional unit hosted by an Area. It provides a specific
interaction experience. It does not know where it is placed; the Area
hosts it.

This module defines the contract only. Concrete Editors may use Qt for
materialization, but the contract itself has no Qt dependency.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any

from cranioz.ui.tools.registry.tool_definition import ToolContext

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from cranioz.ui.editors.editor_context import EditorContext
    from cranioz.ui.toolbars.specification import ToolbarSpec


class Editor(ABC):
    """Abstract Editor contract."""

    editor_id: str
    title: str

    def __init__(self, editor_id: str, title: str) -> None:
        self.editor_id = editor_id
        self.title = title

    # Lifecycle -----------------------------------------------------

    def initialize(self, context: EditorContext) -> None:
        """Called once at insertion. Heavy resources are set up here."""
        return None

    def activate(self) -> None:
        """Called when the Editor becomes the active one in its Area."""
        return None

    def deactivate(self) -> None:
        """Called when the Editor stops being active. Must not dispose."""
        return None

    def dispose(self) -> None:
        """Called when the Editor is removed and its resources released."""
        return None

    # Content -------------------------------------------------------

    @abstractmethod
    def content_widget(self) -> Any:
        """Return the widget that represents the Editor's content."""
        raise NotImplementedError

    def toolbar_specs(self) -> list[ToolbarSpec]:
        """Declare the Editor's internal Toolbars. Empty by default."""
        return []

    def tool_context(self) -> ToolContext:
        """Return the ToolContext used to materialize this Editor's Toolbars.

        The Editor owns its ToolContext; the AreaWidget and the Toolbar
        widget only consume it. Editors that need a richer context
        (selection, scene query, command dispatcher) override this method
        and return an instance with the appropriate services.

        The default implementation returns an empty ``ToolContext``,
        which is correct for Editors that declare no Toolbar or whose
        Tools are context-independent.
        """
        return ToolContext()

    # Persistence ---------------------------------------------------

    def persistent_state(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Return the Editor's persistable presentation state."""
        return {}

    def restore_state(self, state: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        """Restore a previously saved presentation state."""
        return None

"""EditorContext.

Minimal execution context passed to an Editor at initialization.

This is intentionally small. Fields will be added as the UI system
grows. The context is a narrow interface: it does not become a
service locator.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EditorContext:
    """Minimal execution context passed to an Editor."""

    editor_id: str
    extras: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)

======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/editors/editor_registry.py
======================================================================

"""EditorRegistry.

Registers Editor classes by identifier and creates instances.

Rules:
- The Registry registers types, not instances.
- The Registry resolves editor_id to a class (type[Editor]).
- Creating an instance delegates to the Editor's own constructor;
  the caller supplies the editor_id, title, and any extra keyword
  arguments the concrete Editor requires.
- Availability and enablement are not decided here; they are the
  responsibility of the EditorCatalog, which filters registered
  types by context.
- Re-registering the same identifier with the same class is
  idempotent; with a different class it raises ValueError.
- Resolving or creating an unknown identifier raises KeyError.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any

from cranioz.ui.editors.base_editor import Editor


class EditorRegistryError(ValueError):
    """Raised when registration violates a structural rule."""


class EditorRegistry:
    """Registry of Editor classes by identifier."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._types: dict[str, type[Editor]] = {}

    def register(self, editor_id: str, editor_type: type[Editor]) -> None:
        """Register an Editor class under editor_id.

        Idempotent when the same class is registered again.
        Raises EditorRegistryError when a different class is already
        registered under the same identifier, or when editor_id is
        empty.
        """
        if not editor_id:
            raise EditorRegistryError("editor_id must not be empty")
        if editor_id in self._types:
            if self._types[editor_id] is editor_type:
                return
            raise EditorRegistryError(
                f"editor_id already registered with a different type: {editor_id}"
            )
        self._types[editor_id] = editor_type

    def unregister(self, editor_id: str) -> None:
        """Remove a class. No-op if the identifier is not registered."""
        self._types.pop(editor_id, None)

    def resolve(self, editor_id: str) -> type[Editor]:
        """Return the class registered for editor_id.

        Raises KeyError if unknown.
        """
        try:
            return self._types[editor_id]
        except KeyError as exc:
            raise KeyError(f"editor_id not registered: {editor_id}") from exc

    def create(self, id_: str, **kwargs: Any) -> Editor:
        """Create a new Editor instance for the given editor id.

        The first parameter is named `id_` to avoid colliding with a
        keyword argument named `editor_id` that concrete Editors may
        accept in their constructors.

        The caller supplies keyword arguments accepted by the concrete
        Editor constructor. If none are given, the Editor must be
        constructible with no arguments.

        Raises KeyError if unknown.
        """
        editor_type = self.resolve(id_)
        return editor_type(**kwargs)

    def identifiers(self) -> tuple[str, ...]:
        """Return the registered identifiers in registration order."""
        return tuple(self._types.keys())

    def contains(self, editor_id: str) -> bool:
        """Return True if the identifier is registered."""
        return editor_id in self._types

======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/editors/builtin/test_editor.py
======================================================================

"""DummyEditor.

A concrete Editor used for tests and early integration. It is not a
clinical Editor. Its purpose is to exercise the Editor contract, the
ToolbarSpec contract, and the EditorRegistry.

The Editor:
- declares a unique editor_id;
- returns a QLabel as its content widget;
- declares a ToolbarSpec with LogTool and ToggleTool;
- supports persistent_state / restore_state trivially.

This Editor does not touch the Scene, does not issue Commands, and
does not depend on any Module. It exists only to validate the UI
composition machinery.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any

from PySide6.QtWidgets import QLabel, QWidget

from cranioz.ui.editors.base_editor import Editor
from cranioz.ui.editors.editor_context import EditorContext
from cranioz.ui.toolbars.specification import (
    ToolbarOrientation,
    ToolbarSpec,
)
from cranioz.ui.tools.registry.tool_definition import ToolContext


class DummyEditor(Editor):
    """A minimal concrete Editor for tests."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        editor_id: str = "editor.test",
        title: str = "Test Editor",
        toolbar_orientation: ToolbarOrientation = ToolbarOrientation.HORIZONTAL,
    ) -> None:
        super().__init__(editor_id=editor_id, title=title)
        self._toolbar_orientation = toolbar_orientation
        self._label: QLabel | None = None
        self._context: EditorContext | None = None
        self._tool_context = ToolContext()

    # ----- Lifecycle --------------------------------------------------

    def initialize(self, context: EditorContext) -> None:
        self._context = context

    # ----- Content ----------------------------------------------------

    def content_widget(self) -> QWidget:
        if self._label is None:
            self._label = QLabel(self.editor_id)
            self._label.setObjectName(f"dummy_editor.content.{self.editor_id}")
        return self._label

    def toolbar_specs(self) -> list[ToolbarSpec]:
        return [
            ToolbarSpec(
                toolbar_id=f"toolbar.{self.editor_id}",
                title=f"{self.title} Toolbar",
                orientation=self._toolbar_orientation,
                tool_ids=("tool.log", "tool.toggle"),
            )
        ]

    # ----- Persistence ------------------------------------------------

    def persistent_state(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        if self._context is None:
            return {}
        return {
            "editor_id": self.editor_id,
            "extras": dict(self._context.extras),
        }

    def restore_state(self, state: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        # DummyEditor does not keep mutable state to restore.
        return None

    # ----- Convenience for tests -------------------------------------

    def tool_context(self) -> ToolContext:
        return self._tool_context

======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/home/home_page.py
