"""HomePage — the application's entry point and project discovery screen.

The HomePage is a presentation and dispatch layer. It lists projects
and flows, and emits signals for every user intention. It does not
create, delete, open, or close projects; it does not execute flows.
Those operations belong to the Application layer, coordinated by the
MainWindow (piece 7.6b), which listens to the HomePage's signals.

See `home_page.md` §3, §4, §8, §22:

> If an operation changes Project state, the operation does not belong
> to the Homepage.

The HomePage may provide the button; the button emits a signal; the
Application executes.

Layout:

    HomePage
    └── QHBoxLayout
        ├── CommunitySidebar (200 px, hidden by default, full height)
        └── QVBoxLayout
            ├── HomeTopBar       (stretch 8  — ~8%)
            ├── ProjectTable     (stretch 42 — ~42%)
            ├── FlowList         (stretch 42 — ~42%)
            └── HomeBottomBar    (stretch 8  — ~8%)

The right column uses a plain QVBoxLayout with stretch factors,
not a QSplitter. The prototype shows fixed panels, not a draggable
divider. Proportional heights (8/42/42/8) keep the four bars
visually balanced regardless of window size; setMinimumHeight on
the top and bottom bars protects against very small windows.

CommunitySidebar is hidden by default. The ☰ button in the top bar
toggles it. The sidebar is an "extra" resource, not part of the
default workflow. It fills the full height of the HomePage; when
visible, it aligns with the top and bottom edges of the right
column, not just the content above the bottom bar.

StartButton state:

- Disabled (grey) by default.
- Enabled (green) only when BOTH a project and a flow are selected.
- Clicking emits `project_open_requested(project_id, flow_id)`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from PySide6.QtCore import Signal
from PySide6.QtWidgets import (
    QHBoxLayout,
    QVBoxLayout,
    QWidget,
)

from cranioz.application.project.project_catalog import ProjectCatalog
from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId
from cranioz.ui.home.widgets.community_sidebar import CommunitySidebar
from cranioz.ui.home.widgets.flow_list import FlowList
from cranioz.ui.home.widgets.home_bottom_bar import HomeBottomBar
from cranioz.ui.home.widgets.home_top_bar import HomeTopBar
from cranioz.ui.home.widgets.project_table import ProjectTable
from cranioz.ui.home.widgets.start_button import StartButton


class HomePage(QWidget):
    """Entry point of the application."""

    # ----- Top bar ---------------------------------------------------

    help_requested = Signal()
    settings_requested = Signal()

    # ----- Community sidebar -----------------------------------------

    community_requested = Signal()
    library_requested = Signal()
    news_requested = Signal()
    feedback_requested = Signal()
    contribute_requested = Signal()

    # ----- Project table ---------------------------------------------

    new_project_requested = Signal()
    manage_projects_requested = Signal()
    project_selected = Signal(object)  # ProjectId

    # ----- Flow list -------------------------------------------------

    flow_selected = Signal(object)  # str | None
    manage_flows_requested = Signal()

    # ----- Start button ----------------------------------------------

    project_open_requested = Signal(object, str)  # ProjectId, flow_id

    def __init__(
        self,
        catalog: ProjectCatalog,
        parent: QWidget | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        super().__init__(parent)
        self._catalog = catalog
        self._selected_project_id: ProjectId | None = None
        self._selected_flow_id: str | None = None

        self.setObjectName("home_page")

        self._top_bar = HomeTopBar(self)
        self._sidebar = CommunitySidebar(self)
        self._project_table = ProjectTable(catalog, self)
        self._flow_list = FlowList(self)
        self._start_button = StartButton()
        self._bottom_bar = HomeBottomBar(self._start_button, self)

        # ----- Right column: proportional heights 8 / 42 / 42 / 8 ----
        # No QSplitter: the prototype shows fixed panels, not a
        # draggable divider. If per-user proportions are ever needed,
        # that is a separate piece (LayoutState persistence).
        right_column = QWidget(self)
        right_layout = QVBoxLayout(right_column)
        right_layout.setContentsMargins(0, 0, 0, 0)
        right_layout.setSpacing(0)
        right_layout.addWidget(self._top_bar, stretch=8)
        right_layout.addWidget(self._project_table, stretch=42)
        right_layout.addWidget(self._flow_list, stretch=42)
        right_layout.addWidget(self._bottom_bar, stretch=8)

        # ----- Whole page: sidebar (left) | right column ---------------
        # The sidebar uses QSizePolicy.Expanding vertically (see its
        # class), so it fills the full height of the HomePage — not
        # just the height of the content above the bottom bar.
        content = QWidget(self)
        content_layout = QHBoxLayout(content)
        content_layout.setContentsMargins(0, 0, 0, 0)
        content_layout.setSpacing(0)
        content_layout.addWidget(self._sidebar)
        content_layout.addWidget(right_column, stretch=1)

        root = QVBoxLayout(self)
        root.setContentsMargins(0, 0, 0, 0)
        root.setSpacing(0)
        root.addWidget(content)

        self._connect_signals()
        self._sync_start_button()

        # CommunitySidebar is hidden by default. The ☰ button toggles it.
        self._sidebar.setVisible(False)

    # ----- Public API -----------------------------------------------

    def refresh(self) -> None:
        """Reload the project table from the catalog."""
        self._project_table.refresh()

    def selected_project_id(self) -> ProjectId | None:
        return self._selected_project_id

    def selected_flow_id(self) -> str | None:
        return self._selected_flow_id

    # ----- Internals ------------------------------------------------

    def _connect_signals(self) -> None:
        tb = self._top_bar
        tb.toggle_sidebar_requested.connect(self._on_toggle_sidebar)
        tb.help_requested.connect(self.help_requested)
        tb.settings_requested.connect(self.settings_requested)

        sb = self._sidebar
        sb.community_requested.connect(self.community_requested)
        sb.library_requested.connect(self.library_requested)
        sb.news_requested.connect(self.news_requested)
        sb.feedback_requested.connect(self.feedback_requested)
        sb.contribute_requested.connect(self.contribute_requested)

        pt = self._project_table
        pt.new_project_requested.connect(self.new_project_requested)
        pt.manage_projects_requested.connect(self.manage_projects_requested)
        pt.project_selected.connect(self._on_project_selected)

        fl = self._flow_list
        fl.flow_selected.connect(self._on_flow_selected)
        fl.manage_flows_requested.connect(self.manage_flows_requested)

        self._start_button.start_requested.connect(self._on_start_requested)

    def _on_project_selected(self, project_id: ProjectId) -> None:
        self._selected_project_id = project_id
        self.project_selected.emit(project_id)
        self._sync_start_button()

    def _on_flow_selected(self, flow_id: object) -> None:
        self._selected_flow_id = flow_id if isinstance(flow_id, str) else None
        self.flow_selected.emit(self._selected_flow_id)
        self._sync_start_button()

    def _sync_start_button(self) -> None:
        ready = (
            self._selected_project_id is not None
            and self._selected_flow_id is not None
        )
        self._start_button.set_ready(ready)

    def _on_start_requested(self) -> None:
        if self._selected_project_id is None or self._selected_flow_id is None:
            return  # defensive; button should not be enabled
        self.project_open_requested.emit(
            self._selected_project_id, self._selected_flow_id
        )

    def _on_toggle_sidebar(self) -> None:
        self._sidebar.setVisible(not self._sidebar.isVisible())

    # ----- Convenience for tests ------------------------------------

    def _top_bar_widget(self) -> HomeTopBar:
        return self._top_bar

    def _sidebar_widget(self) -> CommunitySidebar:
        return self._sidebar

    def _project_table_widget(self) -> ProjectTable:
        return self._project_table

    def _flow_list_widget(self) -> FlowList:
        return self._flow_list

    def _bottom_bar_widget(self) -> HomeBottomBar:
        return self._bottom_bar

    def _start_button_widget(self) -> StartButton:
        return self._start_button



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/home/__init__.py
======================================================================

"""Home Page — the entry point and project discovery screen."""



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/home/widgets/community_sidebar.py
======================================================================

"""CommunitySidebar — the left vertical panel of the Home Page.

Displays five community-related actions. Each emits a signal; the
HomePage re-emits it; the MainWindow opens the appropriate URL or
dialog.

Visibility:
- The sidebar is hidden by default. The ☰ button in the HomeTopBar
  toggles it. It is an "extra" resource, not part of the default
  workflow.
- It fills the full height of the HomePage (vertical QSizePolicy =
  Expanding), aligning with the top and bottom edges of the right
  column — not just the content above the bottom bar.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from PySide6.QtCore import Qt, Signal
from PySide6.QtWidgets import (
    QPushButton,
    QSizePolicy,
    QVBoxLayout,
    QWidget,
)


class CommunitySidebar(QWidget):
    """Left vertical panel with community-related actions."""

    community_requested = Signal()
    library_requested = Signal()
    news_requested = Signal()
    feedback_requested = Signal()
    contribute_requested = Signal()

    _ITEMS: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...] = (
        ("Comunidade", "community"),
        ("Biblioteca de objetos", "library"),
        ("Novidades", "news"),
        ("Sugerir melhorias", "feedback"),
        ("Contribuir", "contribute"),
    )

    def __init__(self, parent: QWidget | None = None) -> None:
        super().__init__(parent)
        self.setObjectName("home_page.community_sidebar")
        self.setFixedWidth(200)
        # Expand vertically: fill the full height of the HomePage,
        # not just the content above the bottom bar. Without this,
        # the QHBoxLayout that hosts the sidebar would align it to
        # the top with its natural height (the sum of the buttons).
        self.setSizePolicy(
            QSizePolicy.Policy.Fixed,
            QSizePolicy.Policy.Expanding,
        )

        layout = QVBoxLayout(self)
        layout.setContentsMargins(8, 8, 8, 8)
        layout.setSpacing(2)

        self._buttons: dict[str, QPushButton] = {}
        for label, key in self._ITEMS:
            button = QPushButton(label)
            button.setObjectName(f"home_page.community_sidebar.{key}")
            button.setFlat(True)
            button.setCursor(Qt.CursorShape.PointingHandCursor)
            button.setStyleSheet("text-align: left; padding: 6px 8px;")
            layout.addWidget(button)
            self._buttons[key] = button

        self._buttons["community"].clicked.connect(self.community_requested)
        self._buttons["library"].clicked.connect(self.library_requested)
        self._buttons["news"].clicked.connect(self.news_requested)
        self._buttons["feedback"].clicked.connect(self.feedback_requested)
        self._buttons["contribute"].clicked.connect(self.contribute_requested)

        layout.addStretch(1)

    # ----- Convenience for tests -------------------------------------

    def button_for(self, key: str) -> QPushButton:
        """Return the button with the given key (used by tests)."""
        return self._buttons[key]



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/home/widgets/flow_list.py
======================================================================

"""FlowList — the "Fluxos clínicos" section of the Home Page.

Displays the available Flow definitions (Bloco 9). Until the Flow
Catalog exists, the list is empty and shows a placeholder.

The list supports single selection. Selecting a flow emits
`flow_selected(flow_id)`. Deselecting (or no selection) emits
`flow_selected(None)`. The HomePage uses this to enable the StartButton.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from PySide6.QtCore import Qt, Signal
from PySide6.QtWidgets import (
    QHBoxLayout,
    QLabel,
    QPushButton,
    QScrollArea,
    QVBoxLayout,
    QWidget,
)


class FlowList(QWidget):
    """Clinical flow listing with selection and a manage button."""

    flow_selected = Signal(object)  # str | None
    manage_flows_requested = Signal()

    def __init__(self, parent: QWidget | None = None) -> None:
        super().__init__(parent)
        self.setObjectName("home_page.flow_list")
        self._selected_flow_id: str | None = None
        self._flow_buttons: dict[str, QPushButton] = {}

        title = QLabel("Fluxos clínicos")
        title.setObjectName("home_page.flow_list.title")
        title_font = title.font()
        title_font.setPointSize(12)
        title_font.setBold(True)
        title.setFont(title_font)

        self._manage_button = QPushButton("Gerir Fluxos")
        self._manage_button.setObjectName("home_page.flow_list.manage")
        self._manage_button.clicked.connect(self.manage_flows_requested)

        header = QVBoxLayout()
        header.setContentsMargins(0, 0, 0, 0)
        header_row = QVBoxLayout()
        del header_row  # not used; simple vertical header below

        top = QWidget()
        top_layout = QVBoxLayout(top)
        top_layout.setContentsMargins(8, 8, 8, 0)
        row = QWidget()

        row_layout = QHBoxLayout(row)
        row_layout.setContentsMargins(0, 0, 0, 0)
        row_layout.addWidget(title)
        row_layout.addStretch(1)
        row_layout.addWidget(self._manage_button)
        top_layout.addWidget(row)

        # Scroll area with placeholder.
        self._content = QWidget()
        self._content.setObjectName("home_page.flow_list.content")
        self._content_layout = QVBoxLayout(self._content)
        self._content_layout.setContentsMargins(8, 8, 8, 8)
        self._content_layout.setSpacing(6)

        self._placeholder = QLabel("Nenhum fluxo disponível")
        self._placeholder.setObjectName("home_page.flow_list.placeholder")
        self._placeholder.setAlignment(Qt.AlignmentFlag.AlignCenter)
        self._content_layout.addWidget(self._placeholder)
        self._content_layout.addStretch(1)

        scroll = QScrollArea()
        scroll.setObjectName("home_page.flow_list.scroll")
        scroll.setWidgetResizable(True)
        scroll.setWidget(self._content)

        root = QVBoxLayout(self)
        root.setContentsMargins(0, 0, 0, 0)
        root.setSpacing(0)
        root.addWidget(top)
        root.addWidget(scroll, stretch=1)

    # ----- Public API -----------------------------------------------

    def set_flows(self, flows: list[tuple[str, str]]) -> None:
        """Populate the list with (flow_id, display_name) pairs.

        Clears any existing selection. Used by the Bloco 9 integration;
        today it is called only from tests to simulate content.
        """
        # Remove old flow buttons.
        for button in self._flow_buttons.values():
            button.setParent(None)
            button.deleteLater()
        self._flow_buttons.clear()

        # Remove placeholder if flows exist.
        if flows:
            self._placeholder.setParent(None)
            self._placeholder.deleteLater()

        for flow_id, display_name in flows:
            button = QPushButton(display_name)
            button.setObjectName(f"home_page.flow_list.flow.{flow_id}")
            button.setCheckable(True)
            button.setCursor(Qt.CursorShape.PointingHandCursor)
            button.clicked.connect(
                lambda _=False, fid=flow_id: self._on_flow_clicked(fid)
            )
            self._content_layout.insertWidget(
                self._content_layout.count() - 1, button
            )
            self._flow_buttons[flow_id] = button

        self._selected_flow_id = None
        self.flow_selected.emit(None)

    def selected_flow_id(self) -> str | None:
        """Return the id of the selected flow, or None."""
        return self._selected_flow_id

    def flow_count(self) -> int:
        """Number of flow buttons currently displayed."""
        return len(self._flow_buttons)

    # ----- Internals ------------------------------------------------

    def _on_flow_clicked(self, flow_id: str) -> None:
        # Toggle behavior: clicking the selected flow deselects it.
        if self._selected_flow_id == flow_id:
            self._selected_flow_id = None
            for button in self._flow_buttons.values():
                button.setChecked(False)
        else:
            self._selected_flow_id = flow_id
            for fid, button in self._flow_buttons.items():
                button.setChecked(fid == flow_id)
        self.flow_selected.emit(self._selected_flow_id)



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/home/widgets/home_bottom_bar.py
======================================================================

"""HomeBottomBar — the bottom bar of the Home Page.

A horizontal bar at the bottom of the Home Page's right column. It
contains the StartButton, aligned to the right.

Why a bar and not a floating button:

The StartButton marks the commit of a planning session ("I have
selected a project and a flow; take me to the Workspace"). Placing it
as a floating FAB over the FlowList covered the content and made the
button feel like a layer, not a step. Making it the fourth horizontal
bar of the right column — below TopBar, ProjectTable and FlowList —
keeps the layout symmetric (top bar + bottom bar), and gives the
StartButton a dedicated, non-overlapping region.

Layout:
    ┌────────────┬──────────────────────┐
    │            │ HomeTopBar           │
    │ Community  ├──────────────────────┤
    │ Sidebar    │ ProjectTable         │
    │            ├──────────────────────┤
    │            │ FlowList             │
    │            ├──────────────────────┤
    │            │ HomeBottomBar [→]    │
    └────────────┴──────────────────────┘
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from PySide6.QtCore import Qt
from PySide6.QtWidgets import (
    QHBoxLayout,
    QWidget,
)

from cranioz.ui.home.widgets.start_button import StartButton


class HomeBottomBar(QWidget):
    """Bottom bar of the Home Page, hosting the StartButton."""

    MINIMUM_HEIGHT: int = 56

    def __init__(
        self,
        start_button: StartButton,
        parent: QWidget | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        super().__init__(parent)
        self.setObjectName("home_page.bottom_bar")
        self.setMinimumHeight(self.MINIMUM_HEIGHT)
        self._start_button = start_button
        # addWidget reparents the button and positions it; do NOT
        # call setParent manually (that would fight the layout).

        layout = QHBoxLayout(self)
        layout.setContentsMargins(4, 4, 4, 4)
        layout.setSpacing(0)
        layout.addStretch(1)
        layout.addWidget(self._start_button, alignment=Qt.AlignmentFlag.AlignRight)

    # ----- Convenience ----------------------------------------------

    def start_button(self) -> StartButton:
        """Return the hosted StartButton (used by tests and the HomePage)."""
        return self._start_button

======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/home/widgets/project_table.py
======================================================================

"""ProjectTable — the "Lista de projetos" section of the Home Page.

Displays the ProjectSummaries from the ProjectCatalog. Emits signals
for user actions: selection, new project, manage projects. It does not
create, delete, or open projects; the HomePage re-emits the signals
and the MainWindow (piece 7.6b) executes the operations.

The "Gerir projetos" button replaces the previous "Remover projeto".
Rationale: the HomePage should not expose destructive operations
directly. Project deletion belongs in a management page (settings /
project management, piece 7.6d) where the user has context: archive,
export, import, delete. The HomePage opens that page; it does not
delete a project with one click.

Widget choice: QTableWidget, not QTableView + model. A surgeon
typically has dozens of projects, not thousands. QTableWidget handles
that with zero ceremony. Migration to QTableView + ProjectTableModel
is a separate decision, to be taken only if the list needs
server-side sorting, dynamic filtering, or virtualization.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import datetime

from PySide6.QtCore import Qt, Signal
from PySide6.QtWidgets import (
    QAbstractItemView,
    QHBoxLayout,
    QHeaderView,
    QLabel,
    QLineEdit,
    QPushButton,
    QTableWidget,
    QTableWidgetItem,
    QVBoxLayout,
    QWidget,
)

from cranioz.application.project.project_catalog import ProjectCatalog
from cranioz.domain.project.project_id import ProjectId
from cranioz.domain.project.project_summary import ProjectSummary

_COLUMNS = ("Nome", "ID", "Sexo", "Idade", "Criação do projeto", "Última modificação")


class ProjectTable(QWidget):
    """Project listing with search, create/manage buttons, and selection."""

    new_project_requested = Signal()
    manage_projects_requested = Signal()
    project_selected = Signal(object)  # ProjectId

    def __init__(
        self,
        catalog: ProjectCatalog,
        parent: QWidget | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        super().__init__(parent)
        self._catalog = catalog
        self.setObjectName("home_page.project_table")

        # ----- Header -----
        title = QLabel("Lista de projetos")
        title.setObjectName("home_page.project_table.title")
        title_font = title.font()
        title_font.setPointSize(12)
        title_font.setBold(True)
        title.setFont(title_font)

        self._new_button = QPushButton("Novo projeto")
        self._new_button.setObjectName("home_page.project_table.new")
        self._new_button.clicked.connect(self.new_project_requested)

        self._manage_button = QPushButton("Gerir projetos")
        self._manage_button.setObjectName("home_page.project_table.manage")
        self._manage_button.clicked.connect(self.manage_projects_requested)

        self._search_edit = QLineEdit()
        self._search_edit.setObjectName("home_page.project_table.search")
        self._search_edit.setPlaceholderText("Buscar por nome do projeto ou do paciente")
        self._search_edit.setMaximumWidth(280)
        self._search_edit.textChanged.connect(self.refresh)

        header = QHBoxLayout()
        header.addWidget(title)
        header.addStretch(1)
        header.addWidget(self._new_button)
        header.addWidget(self._manage_button)
        header.addWidget(self._search_edit)

        # ----- Table -----
        self._table = QTableWidget(0, len(_COLUMNS))
        self._table.setObjectName("home_page.project_table.table")
        self._table.setHorizontalHeaderLabels(_COLUMNS)
        self._table.setSelectionBehavior(
            QAbstractItemView.SelectionBehavior.SelectRows
        )
        self._table.setSelectionMode(
            QAbstractItemView.SelectionMode.SingleSelection
        )
        self._table.setEditTriggers(QAbstractItemView.EditTrigger.NoEditTriggers)
        self._table.verticalHeader().setVisible(False)
        self._table.horizontalHeader().setSectionResizeMode(
            0, QHeaderView.ResizeMode.Stretch
        )
        self._table.horizontalHeader().setStretchLastSection(True)
        self._table.itemSelectionChanged.connect(self._on_selection_changed)

        layout = QVBoxLayout(self)
        layout.setContentsMargins(8, 8, 8, 8)
        layout.setSpacing(6)
        layout.addLayout(header)
        layout.addWidget(self._table, stretch=1)

        self.refresh()

    # ----- Public API -----------------------------------------------

    def refresh(self) -> None:
        """Reload the table from the catalog, honoring the current search."""
        query = self._search_edit.text()
        if query.strip():
            summaries = self._catalog.search_projects(query)
        else:
            summaries = self._catalog.list_projects()
        self._populate(summaries)

    def selected_project_id(self) -> ProjectId | None:
        """Return the id of the selected row, or None."""
        selection = self._table.selectionModel()
        rows = selection.selectedRows()
        if not rows:
            return None
        text = self._id_text_at_row(rows[0].row())
        return ProjectId(text) if text else None

    def row_count(self) -> int:
        """Number of rows currently displayed."""
        return self._table.rowCount()

    # ----- Internals ------------------------------------------------

    def _id_text_at_row(self, row: int) -> str:
        item = self._table.item(row, 1)
        if item is None:
            return ""
        return item.text()

    def _populate(self, summaries: tuple[ProjectSummary, ...]) -> None:
        # Clear selection first, to avoid signals during repopulation.
        self._table.clearSelection()
        self._table.setRowCount(len(summaries))
        for row, summary in enumerate(summaries):
            self._set_cell(row, 0, summary.project_name)
            self._set_cell(row, 1, summary.project_id.value)
            self._set_cell(row, 2, _format_sex(summary))
            self._set_cell(row, 3, _format_age(summary))
            self._set_cell(row, 4, _format_datetime(summary.created_at))
            self._set_cell(row, 5, _format_datetime(summary.updated_at))

    def _set_cell(self, row: int, column: int, text: str) -> None:
        item = QTableWidgetItem(text)
        item.setTextAlignment(
            Qt.AlignmentFlag.AlignLeft | Qt.AlignmentFlag.AlignVCenter
        )
        self._table.setItem(row, column, item)

    def _on_selection_changed(self) -> None:
        project_id = self.selected_project_id()
        if project_id is not None:
            self.project_selected.emit(project_id)


# ----- Formatting helpers --------------------------------------------


def _format_sex(summary: ProjectSummary) -> str:
    # The ProjectSummary does not carry sex; the project metadata only
    # has patient_display_name. Column is reserved, filled later when
    # the Patient module provides sex. An em-dash placeholder is
    # honest: it says "not available" rather than "missing".
    del summary
    return "—"


def _format_age(summary: ProjectSummary) -> str:
    # Same rationale as _format_sex.
    del summary
    return "—"


def _format_datetime(value: datetime) -> str:
    return value.strftime("%d/%m/%Y")

FILE: src/cranioz/ui/home/widgets/start_button.py
======================================================================

"""StartButton — the "start planning" floating action button.

The button is a fixed-size circle, positioned by the parent HomePage
in the bottom-right corner. It is disabled (grey) until the user has
selected both a project and a flow. When both are selected, it becomes
green and enabled.

The button does not know about project_id or flow_id. It only emits
`start_requested()` when clicked. The HomePage owns the state and
emits the full `project_open_requested(project_id, flow_id)` signal.

The objectName uses underscores, not dots. Qt Style Sheets treat "."
as a class separator; a QSS selector ``#home_page.start_button``
would not match an objectName of ``"home_page.start_button"``. The
underscore form matches literally. See the QSS documentation on
selector syntax.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from PySide6.QtCore import Qt, Signal
from PySide6.QtWidgets import QPushButton, QWidget

# Colors are inline for the first piece. When the ThemeService gains
# semantic colors (ADR-0003 §2.4: color("primary"), color("disabled")),
# replace these with service lookups.
_GREY = "#9e9e9e"
_GREEN = "#2ecc71"
_WHITE = "#ffffff"


class StartButton(QPushButton):
    """Floating circular button for starting a planning session."""

    start_requested = Signal()

    def __init__(self, parent: QWidget | None = None) -> None:
        super().__init__(parent)
        self.setObjectName("home_page_start_button")
        self.setFixedSize(56, 56)
        self.setCursor(Qt.CursorShape.PointingHandCursor)
        self.setEnabled(False)
        self._apply_style(enabled=False)
        self.clicked.connect(self.start_requested)

    def set_ready(self, ready: bool) -> None:
        """Enable (green) or disable (grey) the button."""
        self.setEnabled(ready)
        self._apply_style(enabled=ready)

    def is_ready(self) -> bool:
        """Return True if the button is currently enabled."""
        return self.isEnabled()

    def _apply_style(self, *, enabled: bool) -> None:
        color = _GREEN if enabled else _GREY
        self.setStyleSheet(
            f"QPushButton#home_page_start_button {{"
            f"  background-color: {color};"
            f"  color: {_WHITE};"
            f"  border: 2px solid {'#27ae60' if enabled else '#7f8c8d'};"
            f"  border-radius: 28px;"
            f"  font-size: 22px;"
            f"  font-weight: bold;"
            f"}}"
        )
        self.setText("→")

"""Sub-widgets that compose the Home Page."""



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/theme/service.py
======================================================================

"""ThemeService.

Provides theme-dependent resources to UI widgets.

In this phase the service is minimal: it exposes the active theme name
and resolves logical icon names into concrete icon identifiers.

Concrete visual styling (colors, fonts, spacing) is applied later via
Qt stylesheets. Widgets should never hardcode colors or icon paths;
they consult the ThemeService instead.

Rules:
- The service is stateless with respect to the application; it only
  reports the active theme and resolves icons.
- The service does not decide *when* the theme changes; that is the
  responsibility of a future settings/theme coordinator.
- The service does not know about individual widgets.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, replace
from enum import Enum


class ThemeName(Enum):
    """Identifier of the active theme."""

    LIGHT = "light"
    DARK = "dark"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ThemeService:
    """Resolves theme-dependent resources for UI widgets.

    The service is immutable. To change theme, create a new instance
    via `with_theme`.
    """

    theme: ThemeName = ThemeName.DARK

    def with_theme(self, theme: ThemeName) -> ThemeService:
        """Return a new ThemeService for the given theme."""
        return replace(self, theme=theme)

    def icon_name(self, logical_name: str) -> str:
        """Return the concrete icon name for the active theme.

        For now this is identity: the logical name is the icon name.
        Future themes may map 'log' to 'log_dark' or 'log_light'.

        Rules:
        - The logical name is what Tools declare in their specs.
        - The concrete name is what the UI loader will look up on disk.
        - The mapping depends only on the active theme.
        """
        if not logical_name:
            raise ValueError("logical_name must not be empty")
        return logical_name

FILE: src/cranioz/ui/theme/__init__.py
======================================================================

"""Theme resources for CranioZ UI."""



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/toolbars/base.py
======================================================================

"""Toolbar widget.

Materializes a ToolbarSpec as a Qt widget. It is internal to an Editor;
the Workspace never hosts one.

Responsibilities:
- Resolve each tool_id declared in the spec against the ToolRegistry.
- Create the concrete Tool instances for each item.
- Display the items in the declared orientation.
- Consume a ThemeService for icon resolution.
- Emit a signal when a Tool is triggered or toggled.

Non-responsibilities:
- The Toolbar does not decide availability or enablement.
  The Tool decides those, based on a ToolContext that the Editor
  provides at materialization time.
- The Toolbar does not mutate the Scene. Tools do, via Commands.
- The Toolbar does not persist state. That is the Editor's job.

This widget is deliberately minimal. It is the first Qt widget in
CranioZ and it exists to validate the contract between:
  ToolbarSpec + ToolRegistry + ThemeService → QWidget
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from PySide6.QtCore import Qt, Signal
from PySide6.QtWidgets import (
    QHBoxLayout,
    QPushButton,
    QVBoxLayout,
    QWidget,
)

from cranioz.ui.theme.service import ThemeService
from cranioz.ui.toolbars.specification import (
    ToolbarOrientation,
    ToolbarSpec,
)
from cranioz.ui.tools.registry.tool_definition import Tool, ToolContext
from cranioz.ui.tools.registry.tool_registry import ToolRegistry


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ToolbarItemBinding:
    """A materialized item, linking a ToolbarItem to a Tool instance."""

    tool_id: str
    widget: QWidget
    tool: Tool | None


class Toolbar(QWidget):
    """Qt widget materializing a ToolbarSpec."""

    tool_triggered = Signal(str)  # tool_id
    tool_toggled = Signal(str, bool)  # tool_id, checked

    def __init__(
        self,
        spec: ToolbarSpec,
        registry: ToolRegistry,
        theme: ThemeService,
        context: ToolContext | None = None,
        parent: QWidget | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        super().__init__(parent)
        self._spec = spec
        self._registry = registry
        self._theme = theme
        self._context = context if context is not None else ToolContext()
        self._bindings: list[ToolbarItemBinding] = []

        self.setObjectName(f"toolbar.{spec.toolbar_id}")
        self.setWindowTitle(spec.title)

        self._layout = (
            QHBoxLayout(self)
            if spec.orientation is ToolbarOrientation.HORIZONTAL
            else QVBoxLayout(self)
        )
        self._layout.setContentsMargins(4, 4, 4, 4)
        self._layout.setSpacing(4)
        self._layout.setAlignment(Qt.AlignmentFlag.AlignLeft)

        for tool_id in spec.tool_ids:
            self._append_tool(tool_id)

    # ----- Public API ------------------------------------------------

    @property
    def spec(self) -> ToolbarSpec:
        return self._spec

    def bindings(self) -> tuple[ToolbarItemBinding, ...]:
        """Return the materialized item bindings, in declaration order."""
        return tuple(self._bindings)

    def tool_ids(self) -> tuple[str, ...]:
        return tuple(b.tool_id for b in self._bindings)

    # ----- Internals -------------------------------------------------

    def _append_tool(self, tool_id: str) -> None:
        tool = self._registry.create(tool_id)
        label = tool.label or tool_id
        icon_name = self._theme.icon_name(tool.icon) if tool.icon else None

        # For this first version we render Tools as buttons with their label.
        # Icon resolution is exercised but not yet drawn (no icon loader).
        button = QPushButton(label, self)
        button.setObjectName(f"toolbar.item.{tool_id}")
        button.setToolTip(tool.description or label)
        if icon_name is not None:
            button.setProperty("icon_name", icon_name)

        if self._is_toggle(tool):
            button.setCheckable(True)
            button.toggled.connect(lambda checked, tid=tool_id: self._on_toggle(tid, checked))
        else:
            button.clicked.connect(lambda _=False, tid=tool_id: self._on_click(tid))

        self._layout.addWidget(button)
        self._bindings.append(ToolbarItemBinding(tool_id=tool_id, widget=button, tool=tool))

    def _is_toggle(self, tool: Tool) -> bool:
        return hasattr(tool, "toggle") and hasattr(tool, "checked")

    def _on_click(self, tool_id: str) -> None:
        binding = self._binding_for(tool_id)
        tool = binding.tool
        if tool is None:
            return
        if not tool.is_available(self._context) or not tool.is_enabled(self._context):
            return
        execute = getattr(tool, "execute", None)
        if callable(execute):
            execute(self._context)
        self.tool_triggered.emit(tool_id)

    def _on_toggle(self, tool_id: str, checked: bool) -> None:
        binding = self._binding_for(tool_id)
        tool = binding.tool
        if tool is None:
            return
        toggle = getattr(tool, "toggle", None)
        if callable(toggle):
            current = toggle()
            # The tool's new state wins.
            checked = bool(current)
        self.tool_toggled.emit(tool_id, checked)

    def _binding_for(self, tool_id: str) -> ToolbarItemBinding:
        for binding in self._bindings:
            if binding.tool_id == tool_id:
                return binding
        raise KeyError(f"tool_id not materialized in this toolbar: {tool_id}")



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/toolbars/item.py
======================================================================

"""ToolbarItem types.

Declarative description of the individual elements that compose a
Toolbar. Items are data; the Toolbar widget materializes them.

Distinct from ToolbarSpec, which describes the Toolbar as a whole
(identity, orientation, tools, visibility policy). ToolbarItem
describes a single element inside the Toolbar.

Rules:
- An item must have a unique identity within its Toolbar.
- The types are frozen; item data does not change at runtime.
- The widget state (enabled, visible) is not part of the item; it
  belongs to the Toolbar widget and to the Tool's availability.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum


class ToolbarItemKind(Enum):
    """Kind of Toolbar item."""

    ACTION = "action"
    TOGGLE = "toggle"
    SEPARATOR = "separator"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ActionItem:
    """A Toolbar item that triggers a single action.

    The action is identified by `tool_id`, resolved by the Toolbar
    widget against the ToolRegistry at materialization time.
    """

    tool_id: str
    label: str
    icon: str | None = None

    @property
    def kind(self) -> ToolbarItemKind:
        return ToolbarItemKind.ACTION

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.tool_id:
            raise ValueError("ActionItem requires a tool_id")
        if not self.label:
            raise ValueError("ActionItem requires a label")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ToggleItem:
    """A Toolbar item whose state is on/off.

    The initial state is `checked`. The Toolbar widget manages the
    current state; the item declares the initial value.
    """

    tool_id: str
    label: str
    icon: str | None = None
    checked: bool = False

    @property
    def kind(self) -> ToolbarItemKind:
        return ToolbarItemKind.TOGGLE

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.tool_id:
            raise ValueError("ToggleItem requires a tool_id")
        if not self.label:
            raise ValueError("ToggleItem requires a label")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Separator:
    """A visual separator between groups of items.

    Separators carry no identity and no action.
    """

    @property
    def kind(self) -> ToolbarItemKind:
        return ToolbarItemKind.SEPARATOR



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/toolbars/specification.py
======================================================================

"""ToolbarSpec.

Declarative description of a Toolbar inside an Editor.

A Toolbar is internal to an Editor; the Workspace never hosts one.
The same Tool may be referenced by multiple Toolbars across Editors.

Two concepts are separated here:

- ToolbarSpec describes *what* a Toolbar is: identity, orientation,
  tools, and its presentation policy.
- ToolbarState (see state.py) describes *whether* a Toolbar is
  currently visible. It is runtime state, not declarative.

Presentation policy is declarative and does not change at runtime.
Runtime visibility is state and does change at runtime.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from enum import Enum


class ToolbarOrientation(Enum):
    """Orientation of a Toolbar."""

    HORIZONTAL = "horizontal"
    VERTICAL = "vertical"


class ToolbarVisibilityMode(Enum):
    """Presentation policy for a Toolbar.

    ALWAYS
        Always visible. The user cannot hide it.

    COLLAPSIBLE
        Visible by default. The user may collapse or expand it
        manually. The preference is persisted across sessions.

    AUTO_HIDE
        Hidden by default. The Editor reveals it when the mouse
        approaches its edge zone (hover-reveal). It hides again
        when the mouse leaves the zone. The user does not toggle
        it manually.

    HIDDEN
        Not visible by default. It can only be revealed by an
        explicit action (a menu, a shortcut, a command).
    """

    ALWAYS = "always"
    COLLAPSIBLE = "collapsible"
    AUTO_HIDE = "auto_hide"
    HIDDEN = "hidden"


class ToolbarSpecError(ValueError):
    """Raised when a ToolbarSpec violates structural rules."""


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ToolbarSpec:
    """Declarative description of a Toolbar."""

    toolbar_id: str
    title: str
    orientation: ToolbarOrientation = ToolbarOrientation.HORIZONTAL
    tool_ids: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    visibility_mode: ToolbarVisibilityMode = ToolbarVisibilityMode.ALWAYS

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.toolbar_id:
            raise ToolbarSpecError("toolbar_id must not be empty")
        if not self.title:
            raise ToolbarSpecError("title must not be empty")



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/toolbars/state.py
======================================================================

"""ToolbarState.

Runtime visibility state of a Toolbar.

A ToolbarSpec declares *how* a Toolbar should behave (its visibility
policy). A ToolbarState records *whether* it is currently visible.

State is per-instance, per-session. It may be persisted via
Editor.persistent_state() when the policy is COLLAPSIBLE.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from cranioz.ui.toolbars.specification import ToolbarVisibilityMode


class ToolbarStateError(ValueError):
    """Raised when a ToolbarState violates structural rules."""


@dataclass
class ToolbarState:
    """Runtime state of a Toolbar instance."""

    toolbar_id: str
    visible: bool = True

    @classmethod
    def initial_for(
        cls,
        toolbar_id: str,
        mode: ToolbarVisibilityMode,
    ) -> ToolbarState:
        """Return the initial state for a given toolbar and visibility mode."""
        if mode is ToolbarVisibilityMode.ALWAYS or mode is ToolbarVisibilityMode.COLLAPSIBLE:
            visible = True
        elif mode is ToolbarVisibilityMode.AUTO_HIDE or mode is ToolbarVisibilityMode.HIDDEN:
            visible = False
        else:
            raise ToolbarStateError(f"unknown visibility mode: {mode!r}")
        return cls(toolbar_id=toolbar_id, visible=visible)

"""ToolSpec.

Declarative descriptor for a Tool, used for registration and discovery.

Different from the Tool Protocol: ToolSpec is data, Tool is behavior.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass


class ToolSpecError(ValueError):
    """Raised when a ToolSpec violates structural rules."""


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ToolSpec:
    """Declarative descriptor for a Tool."""

    identifier: str
    label: str
    description: str = ""
    icon: str | None = None
    category: str | None = None

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.identifier:
            raise ToolSpecError("identifier must not be empty")
        if not self.label:
            raise ToolSpecError("label must not be empty")

"""LogTool.

Minimal Tool used for tests and early integration.
It logs a message when triggered. It does not modify the Scene.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging

from cranioz.ui.tools.registry.tool_definition import ToolContext

_logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class LogTool:
    """A Tool that logs a message."""

    identifier = "tool.log"
    label = "Log"
    description = "Logs a message"
    icon: str | None = "log"
    category: str | None = None

    def __init__(self, message: str = "tool triggered") -> None:
        self.message = message

    def is_available(self, context: ToolContext) -> bool:
        return bool(context.extras.get("available", True))

    def is_enabled(self, context: ToolContext) -> bool:
        return bool(context.extras.get("enabled", True))

    def execute(self, context: ToolContext) -> None:
        _logger.info("%s: %s", self.identifier, self.message)

FILE: src/cranioz/ui/tools/builtin/toggle_tool.py

"""ToggleTool.

Minimal Tool with on/off state, used for tests and early integration.
The state is held by the Tool instance; the toolbar widget reads and
writes it via the Tool's methods.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from cranioz.ui.tools.registry.tool_definition import ToolContext


class ToggleTool:
    """A Tool with on/off state."""

    identifier = "tool.toggle"
    label = "Toggle"
    description = "Toggles a state"
    icon: str | None = "toggle"
    category: str | None = None

    def __init__(self, checked: bool = False) -> None:
        self._checked = checked

    def is_available(self, context: ToolContext) -> bool:
        return bool(context.extras.get("available", True))

    def is_enabled(self, context: ToolContext) -> bool:
        return bool(context.extras.get("enabled", True))

    @property
    def checked(self) -> bool:
        return self._checked

    def toggle(self) -> bool:
        """Flip the state and return the new value."""
        self._checked = not self._checked
        return self._checked

======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/tools/registry/tool_definition.py
======================================================================

"""Tool contract.

A Tool is an interaction contract: it describes what the user can
trigger and when it is available. It does not depend on Qt.

This module lives in ui/tools/registry/ because a Tool is materialized
by a Toolbar, which is a UI component. Tools are declared by Modules,
but their contract is a UI-facing interaction contract.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any, Protocol, runtime_checkable


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ToolContext:
    """Minimal execution context passed to a Tool.

    Concrete fields will be added as the tool system grows. This is
    intentionally small.
    """

    extras: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)


@runtime_checkable
class Tool(Protocol):
    """Tool contract.

    Implementations must expose these attributes and methods.
    No Qt dependency is required by the contract itself.
    """

    identifier: str
    label: str
    description: str
    icon: str | None
    category: str | None

    def is_available(self, context: ToolContext) -> bool: ...

    def is_enabled(self, context: ToolContext) -> bool: ...



======================================================================
FILE: src/cranioz/ui/tools/registry/tool_registry.py
======================================================================

"""ToolRegistry.

Registers Tool implementations by identifier and creates instances.

Rules:
- The Registry registers types, not instances.
- The Registry resolves tool_id to a factory (a zero-argument callable).
- The Registry does not decide availability or enablement; that is
  the responsibility of the Tool itself via is_available and is_enabled,
  given a ToolContext.
- Re-registering the same identifier raises ValueError unless the same
  type is registered again (idempotent) — this avoids silent overrides.
- Resolving or creating an unknown identifier raises KeyError.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Callable

from cranioz.ui.tools.registry.tool_definition import Tool


class ToolRegistryError(ValueError):
    """Raised when registration violates a structural rule."""


ToolFactory = Callable[[], Tool]


class ToolRegistry:
    """Registry of Tool factories by identifier."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._factories: dict[str, ToolFactory] = {}

    def register(self, tool_id: str, factory: ToolFactory) -> None:
        """Register a factory under tool_id.

        Idempotent for the same factory; raises if a different factory
        is already registered under the same identifier.
        """
        if not tool_id:
            raise ToolRegistryError("tool_id must not be empty")
        if tool_id in self._factories:
            if self._factories[tool_id] is factory:
                return
            raise ToolRegistryError(
                f"tool_id already registered with a different factory: {tool_id}"
            )
        self._factories[tool_id] = factory

    def unregister(self, tool_id: str) -> None:
        """Remove a factory. No-op if the identifier is not registered."""
        self._factories.pop(tool_id, None)

    def resolve(self, tool_id: str) -> ToolFactory:
        """Return the factory for tool_id.

        Raises KeyError if unknown.
        """
        try:
            return self._factories[tool_id]
        except KeyError as exc:
            raise KeyError(f"tool_id not registered: {tool_id}") from exc

    def create(self, tool_id: str) -> Tool:
        """Create a new Tool instance for tool_id.

        Raises KeyError if unknown.
        """
        return self.resolve(tool_id)()

    def identifiers(self) -> tuple[str, ...]:
        """Return the registered identifiers in registration order."""
        return tuple(self._factories.keys())

    def contains(self, tool_id: str) -> bool:
        """Return True if the identifier is registered."""
        return tool_id in self._factories
